<p><b>Sri Lanka</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small><a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">UK</a>: </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span></span><span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="/ŋ/: &#39;ng&#39; in &#39;sing&#39;">ŋ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ʃ/: &#39;sh&#39; in &#39;shy&#39;">ʃ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span></span><span class="wrap"> </span>-/</a></span></span>, <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small><a href="/wiki/American_English" title="American English">US</a>: </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/-<span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="/ŋ/: &#39;ng&#39; in &#39;sing&#39;">ŋ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Sri_Lanka.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/ae/En-us-Sri_Lanka.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Sri Lanka.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span>; <a href="/wiki/Sinhala_language" title="Sinhala language">Sinhala</a>: <span lang="si">ශ්&#8205;රී ලංකා</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="ISO 15919 Indic (Sinhala language) transliteration"><i lang="si-Latn">Śrī Laṅkā</i></span> (<small>IPA:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="si-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[ʃriː laŋkaː]</a></span>); <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a>: <span lang="ta">இலங்கை</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="ISO 15919 Indic (Tamil language) transliteration"><i lang="ta-Latn">Ilaṅkai</i></span> (<small>IPA:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ta-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Tamil" title="Help:IPA/Tamil">[ilaŋɡaj]</a></span>)), formerly known as <b>Ceylon</b> and officially the <b>Democratic Socialist Republic of Sri Lanka</b>, is an <a href="/wiki/Island_country" title="Island country">island country</a> in <a href="/wiki/South_Asia" title="South Asia">South Asia</a>. It lies in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Ocean" title="Indian Ocean">Indian Ocean</a>, southwest of the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Bengal" title="Bay of Bengal">Bay of Bengal</a>, separated from the <a href="/wiki/Indian_subcontinent" title="Indian subcontinent">Indian peninsula</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar" title="Gulf of Mannar">Gulf of Mannar</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Palk_Strait" title="Palk Strait">Palk Strait</a>. Sri Lanka shares a <a href="/wiki/Maritime_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Maritime border">maritime border</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Maldives" title="Maldives">Maldives</a> in the south-west and <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> in the north-west.
</p><p>Sri Lanka has a population of around 22 million (2020) and is home to many cultures, languages, and ethnicities. The <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_people" title="Sinhalese people">Sinhalese</a> people form the majority of the nation's population, followed by the <a href="/wiki/Tamils" title="Tamils">Tamils</a>, who are the largest minority group and are concentrated in northern Sri Lanka; both the linguistic groups have played an influential role in the island's history. Other long established groups include the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Moors" title="Sri Lankan Moors">Moors</a>, <a href="/wiki/Burgher_people" title="Burgher people">the Burghers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays" title="Sri Lankan Malays">the Malays</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chinese_people_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Chinese people in Sri Lanka">the Chinese</a>, and the indigenous <a href="/wiki/Vedda" title="Vedda">Vedda</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka's documented history goes back 3,000 years, with evidence of prehistoric human settlements that dates back at least 125,000 years.<sup id="cite_ref-histr_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-histr-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The earliest known <a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Buddhism in Sri Lanka">Buddhist</a> writings of Sri Lanka, known collectively as the <a href="/wiki/P%C4%81li_canon" class="mw-redirect" title="Pāli canon">Pāli canon</a>, date to the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Buddhist_council" title="Fourth Buddhist council">fourth Buddhist council</a>, which took place in 29 BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Also called the <i>Teardrop of India</i>, or the <i>Granary of the East</i>, Sri Lanka's geographic location and deep harbours have made it of great strategic importance, from the earliest days of the ancient <a href="/wiki/Silk_Road" title="Silk Road">Silk Road</a> trade route to today's so-called <a href="/wiki/Maritime_Silk_Road" title="Maritime Silk Road">maritime Silk Road</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-silkr_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-silkr-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Because its location made it a major trading hub, it was already known to both Far Easterners and Europeans as long ago as the <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_period" title="Anuradhapura period">Anuradhapura period</a> (377 BCE–1017 CE). During a period of <a href="/wiki/Crisis_of_the_Sixteenth_Century" title="Crisis of the Sixteenth Century">great political crisis</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kotte" title="Kingdom of Kotte">Kingdom of Kotte</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire" title="Portuguese Empire">Portuguese</a> arrived in Sri Lanka and sought to control the island's maritime trade, with a part of Sri Lanka subsequently becoming a <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Ceylon" title="Portuguese Ceylon">Portuguese possession</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese-Portuguese_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Sinhalese-Portuguese war">Sinhalese-Portuguese war</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Empire" title="Dutch Empire">Dutch</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy" title="Kingdom of Kandy">Kingdom of Kandy</a> took control of those areas. The <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Ceylon" title="Dutch Ceylon">Dutch possessions</a> were then taken by the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a>, who later extended their control over the whole island, <a href="/wiki/British_Ceylon" title="British Ceylon">colonising</a> it from 1815 to 1948. A national movement for political independence arose in the early 20th century, and in 1948, Ceylon became a <a href="/wiki/Dominion" title="Dominion">dominion</a>. The dominion was succeeded by the <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a> named Sri Lanka in 1972. Sri Lanka's more recent history was marred by a 26-year <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Sri Lankan Civil War">civil war</a>, which began in 1983 and ended decisively in 2009, when the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces" title="Sri Lanka Armed Forces">Sri Lanka Armed Forces</a> defeated the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka is a <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a>, ranking 73rd on the <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a>. It is the highest-ranked South Asian nation in terms of development and has the second-highest <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">per capita income</a> in South Asia; however, <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%93present_Sri_Lankan_economic_crisis" title="2019–present Sri Lankan economic crisis">the ongoing economic crisis</a> has resulted in the collapse of the currency, rising inflation, and a humanitarian crisis due to a severe shortage of essentials. It has also led to an eruption of <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests" title="2022 Sri Lankan protests">street protests</a>, with citizens successfully demanding that the president and the government step down.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The island has had a long history of engagement with modern international groups: it is a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation" title="South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation">SAARC</a> and a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Group_of_77" title="Group of 77">G77</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Toponymy">Toponymy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Names_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Names of Sri Lanka">Names of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>In antiquity, Sri Lanka was known to travellers by a variety of names. According to the <i><a href="/wiki/Mah%C4%81va%E1%B9%83sa" title="Mahāvaṃsa">Mahāvaṃsa</a></i>, the legendary <a href="/wiki/Prince_Vijaya" title="Prince Vijaya">Prince Vijaya</a> named the island <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tambapanni" title="Kingdom of Tambapanni">Tambapaṇṇĩ</a> ("<a href="/wiki/Copper_(color)" title="Copper (color)">copper-red</a> hands" or "copper-red earth"), because his followers' hands were reddened by the <a href="/wiki/Red_soil" title="Red soil">red soil</a> of the area where he landed.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-JMS_1997_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JMS_1997-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> In <a href="/wiki/Hindu_mythology" title="Hindu mythology">Hindu mythology</a>, the term <a href="/wiki/Lanka" title="Lanka"><i>Lankā</i></a> ("Island") appears but it is unknown whether it refers to the modern day state. But scholars generally agree that it must have been Sri Lanka because it is so stated in the 5th century Sri Lankan text <a href="/wiki/Mahavamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahavamsa">Mahavamsa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The Tamil term <a href="/wiki/Eelam" title="Eelam">Eelam</a> (<a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a>: <span lang="ta">ஈழம்</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Tamil-language romanization"><i lang="ta-Latn">īḻam</i></span>) was used to designate the whole island in <a href="/wiki/Sangam_literature" title="Sangam literature">Sangam literature</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The island was known <a href="/wiki/Chola_conquest_of_Anuradhapura" title="Chola conquest of Anuradhapura">under Chola rule</a> as <i>Mummudi Cholamandalam</i> ("realm of the <a href="/wiki/Three_Crowned_Kings" title="Three Crowned Kings">three crowned</a> Cholas").<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greek" title="Ancient Greek">Ancient Greek</a> geographers called it <i><a href="/wiki/Taprobana" title="Taprobana">Taprobanā</a></i> (<a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Ancient Greek language">Ancient Greek</a>: <span lang="grc">Ταπροβανᾶ</span>) or <i>Taprobanē</i> (<span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">Ταπροβανῆ</span></span>)<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> from the word <i>Tambapanni</i>. The Persians and Arabs referred to it as <i>Sarandīb</i> (the origin of the word "<a href="/wiki/Serendipity" title="Serendipity">serendipity</a>") from <a href="/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit">Sanskrit</a> <i>Siṃhaladvīpaḥ</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> <i>Ceilão</i>, the name given to Sri Lanka by the Portuguese Empire when it arrived in 1505,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> was transliterated into English as <b>Ceylon</b>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> As a British <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">crown colony</a>, the island was known as Ceylon; it achieved independence as the <a href="/wiki/Dominion_of_Ceylon" title="Dominion of Ceylon">Dominion of Ceylon</a> in 1948.
</p><p>The country is now known in Sinhala as <i><span title="ISO 15919 Indic (Sinhala language) transliteration"><i lang="si-Latn">Śrī Laṅkā</i></span></i> (<a href="/wiki/Sinhala_language" title="Sinhala language">Sinhala</a>: <span lang="si">ශ්&#8205;රී ලංකා</span>) and in Tamil as <i><span title="ISO 15919 Indic (Tamil language) transliteration"><i lang="ta-Latn">Ilaṅkai</i></span></i> (<a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a>: <span lang="ta">இலங்கை</span>, <small>IPA:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ta-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Tamil" title="Help:IPA/Tamil">[iˈlaŋɡaɪ]</a></span>). In 1972, its formal name was changed to "Free, Sovereign and Independent Republic of Sri Lanka". Later, on 7 September 1978, it was changed to the "Democratic Socialist Republic of Sri Lanka".<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> As the name Ceylon still appears in the names of a number of organisations, the Sri Lankan government announced in 2011 a plan to rename all those over which it has authority.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Sri_Lanka" title="History of Sri Lanka">History of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prehistoric_Sri_Lanka">Prehistoric Sri Lanka</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Prehistory_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Prehistory of Sri Lanka">Prehistory of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>The pre-history of Sri Lanka goes back 125,000 years and possibly even as far back as 500,000 years.<sup id="cite_ref-pps_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pps-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The era spans the <a href="/wiki/Palaeolithic" class="mw-redirect" title="Palaeolithic">Palaeolithic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mesolithic" title="Mesolithic">Mesolithic</a>, and early <a href="/wiki/Iron_Age" title="Iron Age">Iron Ages</a>. Among the <a href="/wiki/Paleolithic" title="Paleolithic">Paleolithic</a> human settlements discovered in Sri Lanka, <a href="/wiki/Fa_Hien_Cave" title="Fa Hien Cave">Pahiyangala</a> (37,000 BP), named after the <a href="/wiki/Chinese_people" title="Chinese people">Chinese</a> traveller <a href="/wiki/Bhikkhu" title="Bhikkhu">monk</a> <a href="/wiki/Faxian" title="Faxian">Faxian</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Batadombalena" title="Batadombalena">Batadombalena</a> (28,500 BP);<sup id="cite_ref-btd_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-btd-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Belilena" title="Belilena">Belilena</a> (12,000 BP) are the most important. In these caves, archaeologists have found the remains of <a href="/wiki/Anatomy" title="Anatomy">anatomically</a> modern <a href="/wiki/Humans" class="mw-redirect" title="Humans">humans</a> which they have named <a href="/wiki/Balangoda_Man" title="Balangoda Man">Balangoda Man</a>, and other evidence<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> suggesting that they may have engaged in <a href="/wiki/Agriculture" title="Agriculture">agriculture</a> and kept domestic dogs for driving game.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The earliest inhabitants of Sri Lanka were probably ancestors of the <a href="/wiki/Vedda_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Vedda people">Vedda people</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> an indigenous people numbering approximately 2,500 living in modern-day Sri Lanka.
</p><p>During the protohistoric period (1000–500 BCE) Sri Lanka was culturally united with southern India,<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> and shared the same megalithic burials, <a href="/wiki/Black_and_red_ware_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Black and red ware culture">pottery</a>, iron technology, farming techniques and <a href="/wiki/Megalithic_graffiti_symbols" title="Megalithic graffiti symbols">megalithic graffiti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Seneviratne_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seneviratne-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Karunaratne_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Karunaratne-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> This cultural complex spread from southern India along with Dravidian clans such as the <a href="/wiki/Velir" title="Velir">Velir</a>, prior to the migration of <a href="/wiki/Prakrit" title="Prakrit">Prakrit</a> speakers.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Seneviratne_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Seneviratne-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One of the first written references to the island is found in the Indian <a href="/wiki/Epic_poetry" title="Epic poetry">epic</a> <a href="/wiki/Ramayana" title="Ramayana">Ramayana</a>, which provides details of a kingdom named <i><a href="/wiki/Lanka" title="Lanka">Lanka</a></i> that was created by the divine sculptor <a href="/wiki/Vishwakarma" class="mw-redirect" title="Vishwakarma">Vishvakarma</a> for <a href="/wiki/Kubera" title="Kubera">Kubera</a>, the God of Wealth.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> It is said that Kubera was overthrown by his <a href="/wiki/Rakshasa" title="Rakshasa">rakshasa</a> stepbrother, <a href="/wiki/Ravana" title="Ravana">Ravana</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-parkrsl_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parkrsl-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancient_Sri_Lanka">Ancient Sri Lanka</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Pre_Anuradhapura_period" class="mw-redirect" title="Pre Anuradhapura period">Pre Anuradhapura period</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_period" title="Anuradhapura period">Anuradhapura period</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg/220px-Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg/330px-Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg/440px-Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2094" data-file-height="1426" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ptolemy%27s_Taprobana.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ptolemy%27s_world_map" title="Ptolemy&#39;s world map">Ptolemy's world map</a> of Ceylon, first century CE, in a 1535 publication</div></div></div>
<p>According to the <i><a href="/wiki/Mahavamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahavamsa">Mahāvamsa</a></i>, a <a href="/wiki/Pali" title="Pali">Pāḷi</a> chronicle written in the 5th century CE, the original inhabitants of Sri Lanka are said to be the <a href="/wiki/Yaksha" title="Yaksha">Yakshas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Naga_people_(Lanka)" title="Naga people (Lanka)">Nagas</a>. Ancient cemeteries that were used before 600 <a href="/wiki/Common_Era" title="Common Era">BCE</a> have also been discovered in Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Sinhalese history traditionally starts in 543 BCE with the arrival of <a href="/wiki/Prince_Vijaya" title="Prince Vijaya">Prince Vijaya</a>, a semi-legendary prince who sailed with 700 followers to Sri Lanka, after being expelled from <a href="/wiki/Vanga_Kingdom" title="Vanga Kingdom">Vanga Kingdom</a> (present-day <a href="/wiki/Bengal" title="Bengal">Bengal</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> He established the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tambapanni" title="Kingdom of Tambapanni">Kingdom of Tambapanni</a>, near modern-day <a href="/wiki/Mannar,_Sri_Lanka" title="Mannar, Sri Lanka">Mannar</a>. Vijaya (Singha) is the first of the approximately <a href="/wiki/List_of_Sri_Lankan_monarchs" title="List of Sri Lankan monarchs">189 monarchs of Sri Lanka</a> described in chronicles such as the <i><a href="/wiki/Dipavamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Dipavamsa">Dipavamsa</a></i>, <i>Mahāvaṃsa</i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Culavamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Culavamsa">Cūḷavaṃsa</a></i>, and <i>Rājāvaliya</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Once <a href="/wiki/Prakrit" title="Prakrit">Prakrit</a> speakers had attained dominance on the island, the <a href="/wiki/Mahavamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahavamsa">Mahavamsa</a> further recounts the later migration of royal brides and service castes from the Tamil <a href="/wiki/Pandya_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Pandya Kingdom">Pandya Kingdom</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_Kingdom" title="Anuradhapura Kingdom">Anuradhapura Kingdom</a> in the early historic period.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:242px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg/240px-Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="240" height="160" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg/360px-Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg/480px-Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5143" data-file-height="3429" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Buda_de_Avukana_-_01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Avukana_Buddha_statue" title="Avukana Buddha statue">Avukana Buddha statue</a>, a 12-metre-tall (39&#160;ft) standing Buddha statue from the reign of <a href="/wiki/Dhatusena_of_Anuradhapura" title="Dhatusena of Anuradhapura">Dhatusena of Anuradhapura</a>, 5th century</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_period" title="Anuradhapura period">Anuradhapura period</a> (377 BCE&#160;&#8211;&#32;1017 CE) began with the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_Kingdom" title="Anuradhapura Kingdom">Anuradhapura Kingdom</a> in 380 BCE during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Pandukabhaya_of_Anuradhapura" title="Pandukabhaya of Anuradhapura">Pandukabhaya</a>. Thereafter, Anuradhapura served as the <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital city</a> of the country for nearly 1,400 years.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Ancient Sri Lankans excelled at building certain types of <a href="/wiki/Ancient_constructions_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Ancient constructions of Sri Lanka">structures</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_irrigation_network" title="Sri Lankan irrigation network">tanks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stupa" title="Stupa">dagobas</a> and palaces.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> Society underwent a major transformation during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Devanampiya_Tissa_of_Anuradhapura" title="Devanampiya Tissa of Anuradhapura">Devanampiya Tissa</a>, with the arrival of <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> from India. In 250 BCE,<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_(Buddhist_monk)" title="Mahinda (Buddhist monk)">Mahinda</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Bhikkhu" title="Bhikkhu">bhikkhu</a> and the son of the <a href="/wiki/Maurya_Empire" title="Maurya Empire">Mauryan</a> Emperor <a href="/wiki/Ashoka" title="Ashoka">Ashoka</a> arrived in <a href="/wiki/Mihintale" title="Mihintale">Mihintale</a> carrying the message of Buddhism.<sup id="cite_ref-Macmillan1_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Macmillan1-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> His mission won over the monarch, who embraced the faith and propagated it throughout the <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_people" title="Sinhalese people">Sinhalese population</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-mahav1_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mahav1-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Succeeding kingdoms of Sri Lanka would maintain many <a href="/wiki/Schools_of_Buddhism" title="Schools of Buddhism">Buddhist schools</a> and monasteries and support the propagation of Buddhism into other countries in <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asia" title="Southeast Asia">Southeast Asia</a>. Sri Lankan Bhikkhus studied in India's famous ancient Buddhist University of <a href="/wiki/Nalanda" title="Nalanda">Nalanda</a>, which was destroyed by <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Bakhtiyar_Khilji" class="mw-redirect" title="Muhammad bin Bakhtiyar Khilji">Bakhtiyar Khilji</a>. It is probable that many of the scriptures from Nalanda are preserved in Sri Lanka's many monasteries and that the written form of the <a href="/wiki/Tripi%E1%B9%ADaka" title="Tripiṭaka">Tripiṭaka</a>, including Sinhalese Buddhist literature, were part of the University of Nalanda.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> In 245 BCE, <a href="/wiki/Bhikkhuni" class="mw-redirect" title="Bhikkhuni">bhikkhuni</a> <a href="/wiki/Sanghamitta" title="Sanghamitta">Sanghamitta</a> arrived with the <a href="/wiki/Jaya_Sri_Maha_Bodhi" title="Jaya Sri Maha Bodhi">Jaya Sri Maha Bodhi</a> tree, which is considered to be a sapling from the historical <a href="/wiki/Bodhi_Tree" title="Bodhi Tree">Bodhi Tree</a> under which <a href="/wiki/Gautama_Buddha" class="mw-redirect" title="Gautama Buddha">Gautama Buddha</a> became enlightened.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> It is considered the oldest human-planted tree (with a continuous historical record) in the world. (<a href="/wiki/Bodhi_Vamsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Bodhi Vamsa">Bodhivamsa</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka experienced the first of many foreign invasions during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Suratissa_of_Anuradhapura" title="Suratissa of Anuradhapura">Suratissa</a>, who was defeated by two horse traders named <a href="/wiki/Sena_and_Guttika" title="Sena and Guttika">Sena and Guttika</a> from <a href="/wiki/South_India" title="South India">South India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-mahav1_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mahav1-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The next invasion came immediately in 205 BCE by a <a href="/wiki/Chola" class="mw-redirect" title="Chola">Chola</a> named <a href="/wiki/Elara_(monarch)" class="mw-redirect" title="Elara (monarch)">Elara</a>, who overthrew <a href="/wiki/Asela_of_Anuradhapura" title="Asela of Anuradhapura">Asela</a> and ruled the country for 44 years. <a href="/wiki/Dutugamunu" title="Dutugamunu">Dutugamunu</a>, the eldest son of the southern regional sub-king, <a href="/wiki/Kavan_Tissa,_Prince_of_Ruhuna" title="Kavan Tissa, Prince of Ruhuna">Kavan Tissa</a>, defeated Elara in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vijithapura" title="Battle of Vijithapura">Battle of Vijithapura</a>. During its two and a half millennia of existence, the <a href="/wiki/Sinhala_Kingdom" title="Sinhala Kingdom">Sinhala Kingdom</a> was invaded at least eight times by neighbouring South Indian dynasties such as the <a href="/wiki/Chola_dynasty" title="Chola dynasty">Chola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pandya_dynasty" title="Pandya dynasty">Pandya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chera_dynasty" title="Chera dynasty">Chera</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pallava_dynasty" title="Pallava dynasty">Pallava</a>. There also were incursions by the kingdoms of <a href="/wiki/Kalinga_(historical_region)" title="Kalinga (historical region)">Kalinga</a> (modern <a href="/wiki/Odisha" title="Odisha">Odisha</a>) and from the <a href="/wiki/Malay_Peninsula" title="Malay Peninsula">Malay Peninsula</a> as well.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sigiriya.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Sigiriya.jpg/220px-Sigiriya.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Sigiriya.jpg/330px-Sigiriya.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Sigiriya.jpg/440px-Sigiriya.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sigiriya.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Sigiriya" title="Sigiriya">Sigiriya</a> ("Lion Rock"), a rock fortress and city, built by King <a href="/wiki/Kashyapa_I_of_Anuradhapura" title="Kashyapa I of Anuradhapura">Kashyapa</a> (477–495 CE) as a new more defensible capital. It was also used as a Buddhist monastery after the capital was moved back to Anuradhapura.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Buddhist_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Fourth Buddhist Council">Fourth Buddhist Council</a> of <a href="/wiki/Theravada" title="Theravada">Theravada</a> Buddhism was held at the <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_Maha_Viharaya" title="Anuradhapura Maha Viharaya">Anuradhapura Maha Viharaya</a> in Sri Lanka under the patronage of <a href="/wiki/Valagamba_of_Anuradhapura" title="Valagamba of Anuradhapura">Valagamba of Anuradhapura</a> in 25 BCE. The council was held in response to a year in which the harvests in Sri Lanka were particularly poor and many <a href="/wiki/Bhikkhu" title="Bhikkhu">Buddhist monks</a> subsequently died of starvation. Because the <a href="/wiki/P%C4%81li_Canon" class="mw-redirect" title="Pāli Canon">Pāli Canon</a> was at that time <a href="/wiki/Oral_literature" title="Oral literature">oral literature</a> maintained in several recensions by <i>dhammabhāṇaka</i>s (<a href="/wiki/Dharma" title="Dharma">dharma</a> reciters), the surviving monks recognised the danger of not writing it down so that even if some of the monks whose duty it was to study and remember parts of the Canon for later generations died, the teachings would not be lost.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBuswellLopez2013200_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuswellLopez2013200-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> After the council, <a href="/wiki/Palm-leaf_manuscript" title="Palm-leaf manuscript">palm-leaf manuscripts</a> containing the completed Canon were taken to other countries such as <a href="/wiki/Burma" class="mw-redirect" title="Burma">Burma</a>, <a href="/wiki/Thailand" title="Thailand">Thailand</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cambodia" title="Cambodia">Cambodia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Laos" title="Laos">Laos</a>.
</p><p>Sri Lanka was the first Asian country known to have a female ruler: <a href="/wiki/Anula_of_Anuradhapura" title="Anula of Anuradhapura">Anula of Anuradhapura</a> (r. 47–42 BCE).<sup id="cite_ref-sigiriya12_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sigiriya12-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lankan monarchs undertook some remarkable construction projects such as <a href="/wiki/Sigiriya" title="Sigiriya">Sigiriya</a>, the so-called "Fortress in the Sky", built during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Kashyapa_I_of_Anuradhapura" title="Kashyapa I of Anuradhapura">Kashyapa I of Anuradhapura</a>, who ruled between 477 and 495. The Sigiriya rock fortress is surrounded by an extensive network of ramparts and moats. Inside this protective enclosure were gardens, ponds, pavilions, palaces and other structures.<sup id="cite_ref-Ponnamperuma_2013_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ponnamperuma_2013-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Bandaranayake_1999_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bandaranayake_1999-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 993 CE, the invasion of <a href="/wiki/Chola_dynasty" title="Chola dynasty">Chola emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/Rajaraja_I" title="Rajaraja I">Rajaraja I</a> forced the then Sinhalese ruler <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_V_of_Anuradhapura" title="Mahinda V of Anuradhapura">Mahinda V</a> to flee to the southern part of Sri Lanka. Taking advantage of this situation, <a href="/wiki/Rajendra_Chola_I" title="Rajendra Chola I">Rajendra I</a>, son of Rajaraja I, launched a large invasion in 1017. Mahinda V was captured and taken to India, and the Cholas <a href="/wiki/Chola_conquest_of_Anuradhapura" title="Chola conquest of Anuradhapura">sacked the city of Anuradhapura</a> causing the fall of <a href="/wiki/Anuradhapura_Kingdom" title="Anuradhapura Kingdom">Anuradhapura Kingdom</a>. Subsequently, they moved the capital to <a href="/wiki/Polonnaruwa" title="Polonnaruwa">Polonnaruwa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Siriweera_2004,_p._44_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Siriweera_2004,_p._44-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-classical_Sri_Lanka">Post-classical Sri Lanka</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Polonnaruwa_period" title="Polonnaruwa period">Polonnaruwa period</a> and <a href="/wiki/Transitional_period_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Transitional period of Sri Lanka">Transitional period of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>Following a 17-year-long campaign, <a href="/wiki/Vijayabahu_I_of_Polonnaruwa" title="Vijayabahu I of Polonnaruwa">Vijayabahu I</a> successfully drove the Chola out of Sri Lanka in 1070, reuniting the country for the first time in over a century.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Upon his request, ordained monks were sent from <a href="/wiki/Burma" class="mw-redirect" title="Burma">Burma</a> to Sri Lanka to re-establish Buddhism, which had almost disappeared from the country during the Chola reign.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> During the medieval period, Sri Lanka was divided into three sub-territories, namely, <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Ruhuna" title="Principality of Ruhuna">Ruhunu</a>, Pihiti and <a href="/wiki/Maya_Rata" title="Maya Rata">Maya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg/220px-Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="178" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg/330px-Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg/440px-Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2250" data-file-height="1825" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gal_Viharaya_02.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The seated image of <a href="/wiki/Gal_Vihara" title="Gal Vihara">Gal Vihara</a> in <a href="/wiki/Polonnaruwa" title="Polonnaruwa">Polonnaruwa</a>, 12th century, which depicts the dhyana mudra, shows signs of Mahayana influence.</div></div></div>
<p>Sri Lanka's <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_irrigation_network" title="Sri Lankan irrigation network">irrigation system</a> was extensively expanded during the reign of <a href="/wiki/Parakramabahu_I_of_Polonnaruwa" class="mw-redirect" title="Parakramabahu I of Polonnaruwa">Parākramabāhu the Great</a> (1153–1186).<sup id="cite_ref-ethniccr_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethniccr-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> This period is considered as a time when Sri Lanka was at the height of its power.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> He built 1,470 reservoirs – the highest number by any ruler in Sri Lanka's history – repaired 165 dams, 3,910 canals, 163 major reservoirs, and 2,376 mini-reservoirs.<sup id="cite_ref-ethniccr1_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethniccr1-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> His most famous construction is the <a href="/wiki/Parakrama_Samudra" title="Parakrama Samudra">Parakrama Samudra</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> the largest irrigation project of medieval Sri Lanka. Parākramabāhu's reign is memorable for two major campaigns – in the south of India as part of a Pandyan war of succession, and a punitive strike against the kings of Ramanna (<a href="/wiki/Myanmar" title="Myanmar">Burma</a>) for various perceived insults to Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After his demise, Sri Lanka gradually decayed in power. In 1215, <a href="/wiki/Kalinga_Magha" title="Kalinga Magha">Kalinga Magha</a>, an invader with uncertain origins, identified as the founder of the Jaffna kingdom, invaded and captured the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Polonnaruwa" title="Kingdom of Polonnaruwa">Kingdom of Polonnaruwa</a>. He sailed from <a href="/wiki/Kalinga_(historical_region)" title="Kalinga (historical region)">Kalinga</a><sup id="cite_ref-ethniccr1_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethniccr1-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> 690 nautical miles on 100 large ships with a 24,000 strong army. Unlike previous invaders, he <a href="/wiki/Looting" title="Looting">looted</a>, ransacked and destroyed everything in the ancient Anuradhapura and Polonnaruwa Kingdoms beyond recovery.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> His priorities in ruling were to extract as much as possible from the land and overturn as many of the traditions of <a href="/wiki/Rajarata" title="Rajarata">Rajarata</a> as possible. His reign saw the massive migration of native <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_people" title="Sinhalese people">Sinhalese people</a> to the south and west of Sri Lanka, and into the mountainous interior, in a bid to escape his power.<sup id="cite_ref-Nadarajan,_V_p._72_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nadarajan,_V_p._72-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Indrapala,_K_p._16_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Indrapala,_K_p._16-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka never really recovered from the impact of Kalinga Magha's invasion. King Vijayabâhu III, who led the resistance, brought the kingdom to <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dambadeniya" title="Kingdom of Dambadeniya">Dambadeniya</a>. The north, in the meanwhile, eventually evolved into the <a href="/wiki/Jaffna_kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Jaffna kingdom">Jaffna kingdom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Nadarajan,_V_p._72_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nadarajan,_V_p._72-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Indrapala,_K_p._16_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Indrapala,_K_p._16-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> The Jaffna kingdom never came under the rule of any kingdom of the south except on one occasion; in 1450, following the conquest led by king <a href="/wiki/Parakramabahu_VI_of_Kotte" title="Parakramabahu VI of Kotte">Parâkramabâhu VI</a>'s adopted son, <a href="/wiki/Bhuvanaikabahu_VI_of_Kotte" title="Bhuvanaikabahu VI of Kotte">Prince Sapumal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> He ruled the North from 1450 to 1467 CE.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The next three centuries starting from 1215 were marked by kaleidoscopically shifting collections of capitals in south and central Sri Lanka, including Dambadeniya, <a href="/wiki/Yapahuwa" title="Yapahuwa">Yapahuwa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gampola" title="Gampola">Gampola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Raigama" class="mw-redirect" title="Raigama">Raigama</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kotte" title="Kingdom of Kotte">Kotte</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-HC_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HC-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sitawaka" title="Kingdom of Sitawaka">Sitawaka</a>, and finally, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy" title="Kingdom of Kandy">Kandy</a>. In 1247, the Malay kingdom of <a href="/wiki/Tambralinga" title="Tambralinga">Tambralinga</a> which was a vassal of the <a href="/wiki/Srivijaya_Empire" class="mw-redirect" title="Srivijaya Empire">Srivijaya Empire</a> led by their king <a href="/wiki/Chandrabhanu" title="Chandrabhanu">Chandrabhanu</a><sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> briefly invaded Sri Lanka from <a href="/wiki/Insular_Southeast_Asia" class="mw-redirect" title="Insular Southeast Asia">Insular Southeast Asia</a>. They were then expelled by the South Indian Pandyan Dynasty.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> However, this temporary invasion reinforced the steady flow of the presence of various <a href="/wiki/Austronesian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Austronesian people">Austronesian</a> merchant ethnic groups, from <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays" title="Sri Lankan Malays">Sumatrans</a> (Indonesia) to <a href="/wiki/Philippines%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations" title="Philippines–Sri Lanka relations">Lucoes</a> (Philippines) into Sri Lanka which occurred since 200 BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Chinese admiral <a href="/wiki/Zheng_He" title="Zheng He">Zheng He</a> and his naval expeditionary force landed at Galle, Sri Lanka in 1409 and got into <a href="/wiki/Ming-Kotte_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Ming-Kotte War">battle</a> with the local king <a href="/wiki/Vira_Alakesvara_of_Gampola" title="Vira Alakesvara of Gampola">Vira Alakesvara of Gampola</a>. Zheng He captured King Vira Alakesvara and later released him.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Zheng He erected the <a href="/wiki/Galle_Trilingual_Inscription" title="Galle Trilingual Inscription">Galle Trilingual Inscription</a>, a stone tablet at <a href="/wiki/Galle" title="Galle">Galle</a> written in three <a href="/wiki/Languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Languages">languages</a> (<a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Persian_language" title="Persian language">Persian</a>), to commemorate his visit.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Stele" title="Stele">stele</a> was discovered by S. H. Thomlin at Galle in 1911 and is now preserved in the <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_Colombo" class="mw-redirect" title="National Museum of Colombo">Colombo National Museum</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_Modern_Sri_Lanka">Early Modern Sri Lanka</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_period" title="Kandyan period">Kandyan period</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Ceylon" title="Portuguese Ceylon">Portuguese Ceylon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Ceylon" title="Dutch Ceylon">Dutch Ceylon</a>, and <a href="/wiki/British_Ceylon_period" title="British Ceylon period">British Ceylon period</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SpilbergenVimala.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/SpilbergenVimala.jpg/220px-SpilbergenVimala.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/SpilbergenVimala.jpg/330px-SpilbergenVimala.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/SpilbergenVimala.jpg/440px-SpilbergenVimala.jpg 2x" data-file-width="711" data-file-height="479" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SpilbergenVimala.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A 17th-century engraving of Dutch explorer <a href="/wiki/Joris_van_Spilbergen" title="Joris van Spilbergen">Joris van Spilbergen</a> meeting with King Vimaladharmasuriya in 1602</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_(Cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="A 1595 map of Sri Lanka created by Dutch cartographer Petrus Plancius" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg/220px-Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg/330px-Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg/440px-Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_%28Cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="11929" data-file-height="8297" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Laor_Sri_Lanka_Map_(Cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A 1595 map of Sri Lanka created by Dutch cartographer <a href="/wiki/Petrus_Plancius" title="Petrus Plancius">Petrus Plancius</a></div></div></div>
<p>The early modern period of Sri Lanka begins with the arrival of <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portuguese</a> soldier and explorer <a href="/wiki/Louren%C3%A7o_de_Almeida" title="Lourenço de Almeida">Lourenço de Almeida</a>, the son of <a href="/wiki/Francisco_de_Almeida" title="Francisco de Almeida">Francisco de Almeida</a>, in 1505.<sup id="cite_ref-francal_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-francal-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> In 1517, the Portuguese built a fort at the port city of <a href="/wiki/Colombo" title="Colombo">Colombo</a> and gradually extended their control over the coastal areas. In 1592, after decades of intermittent warfare with the Portuguese, <a href="/wiki/Vimaladharmasuriya_I_of_Kandy" title="Vimaladharmasuriya I of Kandy">Vimaladharmasuriya I</a> moved his kingdom to the inland city of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy" title="Kingdom of Kandy">Kandy</a>, a location he thought more secure from attack.<sup id="cite_ref-isl23_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-isl23-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> In 1619, succumbing to attacks by the Portuguese, the independent existence of the <a href="/wiki/Jaffna_kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Jaffna kingdom">Jaffna kingdom</a> came to an end.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the reign of the <a href="/wiki/Rajasinha_II_of_Kandy" title="Rajasinha II of Kandy">Rajasinha II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch</a> explorers arrived on the island. In 1638, the king signed a <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_Treaty_of_1638" title="Kandyan Treaty of 1638">treaty</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company" title="Dutch East India Company">Dutch East India Company</a> to get rid of the Portuguese who ruled most of the coastal areas.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The following <a href="/wiki/Dutch%E2%80%93Portuguese_War" title="Dutch–Portuguese War">Dutch–Portuguese War</a> resulted in a Dutch victory, with Colombo falling into Dutch hands by 1656. The Dutch remained in the areas they had captured, thereby violating the treaty they had signed in 1638. The <a href="/wiki/Burgher_people" title="Burgher people">Burgher people</a>, a distinct ethnic group, emerged as a result of intermingling between the Dutch and native Sri Lankans in this period.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Kingdom of Kandy was the last independent monarchy of Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-kandyk_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kandyk-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> In 1595, Vimaladharmasurya brought the sacred <a href="/wiki/Relic_of_the_tooth_of_the_Buddha" title="Relic of the tooth of the Buddha">Tooth Relic</a>—the traditional symbol of royal and religious authority amongst the <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_people" title="Sinhalese people">Sinhalese</a>—to Kandy and built the <a href="/wiki/Temple_of_the_Tooth" title="Temple of the Tooth">Temple of the Tooth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-kandyk_98-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kandyk-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> In spite of on-going intermittent warfare with Europeans, the kingdom survived. Later, a crisis of succession emerged in Kandy upon king <a href="/wiki/Vira_Narendra_Sinha_of_Sri_Lanka" class="mw-redirect" title="Vira Narendra Sinha of Sri Lanka">Vira Narendrasinha</a>'s death in 1739. He was married to a <a href="/wiki/Telugu_language" title="Telugu language">Telugu</a>-speaking <a href="/wiki/Nayaks_of_Madurai" class="mw-redirect" title="Nayaks of Madurai">Nayakkar</a> princess from South India (<a href="/wiki/Madurai" title="Madurai">Madurai</a>) and was childless by her.<sup id="cite_ref-kandyk_98-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kandyk-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eventually, with the support of <a href="/wiki/Bhikku" class="mw-redirect" title="Bhikku">bhikku</a> Weliwita Sarankara and ignoring the right of <i>"Unambuwe Bandara"</i>, the crown passed to the brother of one of Narendrasinha's princesses, overlooking Narendrasinha's own son by a Sinhalese <a href="/wiki/Concubinage" title="Concubinage">concubine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The new king was crowned <a href="/wiki/Vijaya_Rajasinha_of_Kandy" class="mw-redirect" title="Vijaya Rajasinha of Kandy">Sri Vijaya Rajasinha</a> later that year. Kings of the Nayakkar dynasty launched several attacks on Dutch controlled areas, which proved to be unsuccessful.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha.jpg/220px-Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="342" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/ea/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="288" data-file-height="448" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha_of_Kandy" title="Sri Vikrama Rajasinha of Kandy">Sri Vikrama Rajasinha of Kandy</a>, the last ruling native Sri Lankan monarch</div></div></div>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a>, fearing that French control of the Netherlands might deliver Sri Lanka to the French, Great Britain occupied the coastal areas of the island (which they <a href="/wiki/Geographical_renaming" title="Geographical renaming">called</a> Ceylon) with little difficulty in 1796.<sup id="cite_ref-colvoyg_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-colvoyg-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Two years later, in 1798, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Rajadhi_Rajasinha_of_Kandy" title="Sri Rajadhi Rajasinha of Kandy">Sri Rajadhi Rajasinha</a>, third of the four Nayakkar kings of Sri Lanka, died of a fever. Following his death, a nephew of Rajadhi Rajasinha, eighteen-year-old Kannasamy, was crowned.<sup id="cite_ref-scnsl_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scnsl-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The young king, now named <a href="/wiki/Sri_Vikrama_Rajasinha_of_Kandy" title="Sri Vikrama Rajasinha of Kandy">Sri Vikrama Rajasinha</a>, faced a <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a> invasion in 1803 but successfully retaliated. The First Kandyan War ended in a stalemate.<sup id="cite_ref-scnsl_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scnsl-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By then the entire coastal area was under the <a href="/wiki/British_East_India_Company" class="mw-redirect" title="British East India Company">British East India Company</a> as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Amiens" title="Treaty of Amiens">Treaty of Amiens</a>. On 14 February 1815, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kandy" title="Kingdom of Kandy">Kandy</a> was occupied by the British in the <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_Wars" title="Kandyan Wars">second Kandyan War</a>, ending Sri Lanka's independence.<sup id="cite_ref-scnsl_102-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scnsl-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Sri Vikrama Rajasinha, the last native monarch of Sri Lanka, was exiled to India.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_Convention" title="Kandyan Convention">Kandyan Convention</a> formally ceded the entire country to the British Empire. Attempts by Sri Lankan noblemen to undermine British power in 1818 during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Rebellion_of_1817%E2%80%931818" title="Great Rebellion of 1817–1818">Uva Rebellion</a> were thwarted by <a href="/wiki/British_governors_of_Ceylon" class="mw-redirect" title="British governors of Ceylon">Governor</a> <a href="/wiki/Robert_Brownrigg" title="Robert Brownrigg">Robert Brownrigg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-keppetipola_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-keppetipola-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The beginning of the modern period of Sri Lanka is marked by the <a href="/wiki/Colebrooke-Cameron_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Colebrooke-Cameron Commission">Colebrooke-Cameron reforms</a> of 1833.<sup id="cite_ref-corankel_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-corankel-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> They introduced a <a href="/wiki/Utilitarianism" title="Utilitarianism">utilitarian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Liberalism" title="Liberalism">liberal</a> political culture to the country based on the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_law" title="Rule of law">rule of law</a> and amalgamated the Kandyan and maritime provinces as a single unit of government.<sup id="cite_ref-corankel_105-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-corankel-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Ceylon" title="Executive Council of Ceylon">executive council</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Ceylon" title="Legislative Council of Ceylon">legislative council</a> were established, later becoming the foundation of a representative legislature. By this time, experiments with <a href="/wiki/Coffee" title="Coffee">coffee</a> plantations were largely successful.<sup id="cite_ref-Nubin_2002_115_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nubin_2002_115-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Soon, coffee became the primary commodity export of Sri Lanka. Falling coffee prices as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Panic_of_1847" title="Panic of 1847">depression of 1847</a> stalled economic development and prompted the governor to introduce a series of taxes on firearms, dogs, shops, boats, etc., and to reintroduce a form of <i>rajakariya</i>, requiring six days free labour on roads or payment of a cash equivalent.<sup id="cite_ref-Nubin_2002_115_106-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nubin_2002_115-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> These harsh measures antagonised the locals, and <a href="/wiki/Matale_Rebellion" class="mw-redirect" title="Matale Rebellion">another rebellion</a> broke out in 1848.<sup id="cite_ref-matale1_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-matale1-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> A devastating leaf disease, <i><a href="/wiki/Hemileia_vastatrix" title="Hemileia vastatrix">Hemileia vastatrix</a></i>, struck the coffee plantations in 1869, destroying the entire industry within fifteen years.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> The British quickly found a replacement: abandoning coffee, they began cultivating tea instead. <a href="/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Tea production in Sri Lanka">Tea production in Sri Lanka</a> thrived in the following decades. Large-scale rubber plantations began in the early 20th century.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:312px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/e/e0/Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG/310px-Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG" decoding="async" width="310" height="183" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/e/e0/Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG/465px-Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/e/e0/Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG/620px-Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1006" data-file-height="593" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kandyan_Chiefs_in_1905.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>British appointed <a href="/wiki/Native_headmen_of_Ceylon" title="Native headmen of Ceylon">Kandyan chief headmen</a> in 1905.</div></div></div>
<p>By the end of the 19th century, a new educated <a href="/wiki/Social_class" title="Social class">social class</a> transcending race and <a href="/wiki/Caste" title="Caste">caste</a> arose through British attempts to staff the <a href="/wiki/Ceylon_Civil_Service" title="Ceylon Civil Service">Ceylon Civil Service</a> and the legal, educational, engineering, and medical professions with natives.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> New leaders represented the various ethnic groups of the population in the <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Council_of_Ceylon" title="Legislative Council of Ceylon">Ceylon Legislative Council</a> on a communal basis. Buddhist and Hindu revivalism reacted against <a href="/wiki/Mission_(Christianity)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mission (Christianity)">Christian missionary</a> activities.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tamlwk_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tamlwk-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The first two decades in the 20th century are noted by the unique harmony among Sinhalese and <a href="/wiki/Tamils" title="Tamils">Tamil</a> political leadership, which has since been lost.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/1906_malaria_outbreak_in_Ceylon" title="1906 malaria outbreak in Ceylon">1906 malaria outbreak in Ceylon</a> actually started in the early 1900s, but the first case was documented in 1906.
</p><p>In 1919, major Sinhalese and Tamil political organisations united to form the Ceylon National Congress, under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Ponnambalam_Arunachalam" title="Ponnambalam Arunachalam">Ponnambalam Arunachalam</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> pressing colonial masters for more constitutional reforms. But without massive popular support, and with the governor's encouragement for "communal representation" by creating a "Colombo seat" that dangled between Sinhalese and Tamils, the Congress lost momentum towards the mid-1920s.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Donoughmore_Constitution" title="Donoughmore Constitution">Donoughmore reforms</a> of 1931 repudiated the communal representation and introduced <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal adult franchise</a> (the franchise stood at 4% before the reforms). This step was strongly criticised by the Tamil political leadership, who realised that they would be reduced to a minority in the newly created <a href="/wiki/State_Council_of_Ceylon" title="State Council of Ceylon">State Council of Ceylon</a>, which succeeded the legislative council.<sup id="cite_ref-tamllib_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tamllib-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> In 1937, Tamil leader <a href="/wiki/G._G._Ponnambalam" title="G. G. Ponnambalam">G. G. Ponnambalam</a> demanded a 50–50 representation (50% for the Sinhalese and 50% for other ethnic groups) in the State Council. However, this demand was not met by the <a href="/wiki/Soulbury_Commission" title="Soulbury Commission">Soulbury reforms</a> of 1944–45.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Contemporary_Sri_Lanka">Contemporary Sri Lanka</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Sri_Lanka_(1948%E2%80%93present)" title="History of Sri Lanka (1948–present)">History of Sri Lanka (1948–present)</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_independence_movement" title="Sri Lankan independence movement">Sri Lankan independence movement</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Sri Lankan Civil War">Sri Lankan Civil War</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SL_Independence.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/SL_Independence.jpg/220px-SL_Independence.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="156" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/SL_Independence.jpg/330px-SL_Independence.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/SL_Independence.jpg/440px-SL_Independence.jpg 2x" data-file-width="550" data-file-height="389" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SL_Independence.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The formal ceremony marking the start of self-rule, with the opening of the first parliament at <a href="/wiki/Independence_Square,_Colombo" class="mw-redirect" title="Independence Square, Colombo">Independence Square</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Soulbury constitution ushered in <a href="/wiki/Dominion_of_Ceylon" title="Dominion of Ceylon">dominion status</a>, with independence proclaimed on 4 February 1948.<sup id="cite_ref-countrst68_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-countrst68-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Don_Stephen_Senanayake" class="mw-redirect" title="Don Stephen Senanayake">D. S. Senanayake</a> became the first <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Prime Minister of Sri Lanka">Prime Minister of Ceylon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DS_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DS-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Prominent <a href="/wiki/Tamils" title="Tamils">Tamil</a> leaders including Ponnambalam and <a href="/wiki/Arunachalam_Mahadeva" title="Arunachalam Mahadeva">Arunachalam Mahadeva</a> joined his cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-tamllib_115-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tamllib-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">British Royal Navy</a> remained stationed at <a href="/wiki/Trincomalee" title="Trincomalee">Trincomalee</a> until 1956. A countrywide <a href="/wiki/1953_Ceylonese_Hartal" title="1953 Ceylonese Hartal">popular demonstration</a> against withdrawal of the rice rations resulted in the resignation of prime minister <a href="/wiki/Dudley_Senanayake" title="Dudley Senanayake">Dudley Senanayake</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-islnksd_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islnksd-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike" title="S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike">S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike</a> was elected prime minister in 1956. His three-year rule had a profound impact through his self-proclaimed role of "defender of the besieged Sinhalese culture".<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> He introduced the controversial <a href="/wiki/Sinhala_Only_Act" title="Sinhala Only Act">Sinhala Only Act</a>, recognising <a href="/wiki/Sinhala_language" title="Sinhala language">Sinhala</a> as the only official language of the government. Although partially reversed in 1958, the bill posed a grave concern for the Tamil community, which perceived in it a threat to their language and culture.<sup id="cite_ref-FW_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FW-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AV_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AV-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-a1_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-a1-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Illankai_Tamil_Arasu_Kachchi" class="mw-redirect" title="Illankai Tamil Arasu Kachchi">Federal Party</a> (FP) launched a movement of non-violent resistance (<a href="/wiki/Satyagraha" title="Satyagraha">satyagraha</a>) against the bill, which prompted Bandaranaike to reach an agreement (<a href="/wiki/Bandaranaike%E2%80%93Chelvanayakam_Pact" title="Bandaranaike–Chelvanayakam Pact">Bandaranaike–Chelvanayakam Pact</a>) with <a href="/wiki/S._J._V._Chelvanayakam" title="S. J. V. Chelvanayakam">S. J. V. Chelvanayakam</a>, leader of the FP, to resolve the looming ethnic conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The pact proved ineffective in the face of ongoing protests by opposition and the Buddhist clergy. The bill, together with various government <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_state-sponsored_colonisation_schemes" title="Sri Lankan state-sponsored colonisation schemes">colonisation schemes</a>, contributed much towards the political rancour between Sinhalese and Tamil political leaders.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Bandaranaike was <a href="/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike#Assassination" title="S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike">assassinated</a> by an extremist Buddhist monk in 1959.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-left{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-right{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-center{text-align:center}@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbinner{width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:none!important;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{justify-content:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{float:none!important;max-width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:212px;max-width:212px"><div class="trow"><div class="theader">Leaders in 1960</div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:107px;max-width:107px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:124px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Her_Majesty_The_Queen_(1959).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg/105px-Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="105" height="124" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg/158px-Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg/210px-Her_Majesty_The_Queen_%281959%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="515" data-file-height="607" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II" title="Elizabeth II">Elizabeth II</a><br /><small>Queen of Ceylon</small></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:101px;max-width:101px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:124px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Sirimavo_Bandaranaike,_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_(cropped).PNG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG/99px-Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG" decoding="async" width="99" height="124" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG/149px-Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG/198px-Sirimavo_Bandaranaike%2C_Prime_Minister_of_Ceylon_1960_%28cropped%29.PNG 2x" data-file-width="270" data-file-height="339" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike" title="Sirimavo Bandaranaike">Sirimavo Bandaranaike</a><br /><small>Prime Minister</small></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div class="thumbcaption">1960 saw the election of Sirimavo Bandaranaike as Ceylon's Prime Minister and the first time in world history that the heads of both state and government in a country were female.</div></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike" title="Sirimavo Bandaranaike">Sirimavo Bandaranaike</a>, the widow of Bandaranaike, took office as prime minister in 1960, and withstood an <a href="/wiki/1962_Ceylonese_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat_attempt" title="1962 Ceylonese coup d&#39;état attempt">attempted coup d'état</a> in 1962. During her second term as prime minister, the government instituted socialist economic policies, strengthening ties with the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> and China, while promoting a policy of non-alignment. In 1971, Ceylon experienced a <a href="/wiki/1971_JVP_insurrection" title="1971 JVP insurrection">Marxist insurrection</a>, which was quickly suppressed. In 1972, the country became a <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a> named Sri Lanka, repudiating its dominion status. Prolonged minority grievances and the use of communal emotionalism as an election campaign weapon by both Sinhalese and Tamil leaders abetted a fledgling Tamil militancy in the north during the 1970s.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Policy_of_standardisation" title="Policy of standardisation">policy of standardisation</a> by the Sirimavo government to rectify disparities created in university enrollment, which was in essence an <a href="/wiki/Affirmative_action" title="Affirmative action">affirmative action</a> to assist geographically disadvantaged students to obtain tertiary education,<sup id="cite_ref-affrmact_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-affrmact-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> resulted in reducing the proportion of Tamil students at university level and acted as the immediate catalyst for the rise of militancy.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The assassination of <a href="/wiki/Jaffna" title="Jaffna">Jaffna</a> <a href="/wiki/Mayor" title="Mayor">Mayor</a> <a href="/wiki/Alfred_Duraiyappah" class="mw-redirect" title="Alfred Duraiyappah">Alfred Duraiyappah</a> in 1975 by the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam</a> (LTTE) marked a crisis point.<sup id="cite_ref-Hoffman139_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hoffman139-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-rohang_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rohang-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of <a href="/wiki/J._R._Jayawardene" class="mw-redirect" title="J. R. Jayawardene">J. R. Jayawardene</a> swept to power in 1977, defeating the largely unpopular <a href="/wiki/United_Front_(Sri_Lanka)" title="United Front (Sri Lanka)">United Front</a> government.<sup id="cite_ref-attms_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-attms-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Jayawardene introduced a <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Constitution of Sri Lanka">new constitution</a>, together with a <a href="/wiki/Free-market_economy" class="mw-redirect" title="Free-market economy">free-market economy</a> and a powerful <a href="/wiki/Executive_president" title="Executive president">executive presidency</a> modelled after that of France. It made Sri Lanka the first <a href="/wiki/South_Asia" title="South Asia">South Asian</a> country to <a href="/wiki/Liberalism" title="Liberalism">liberalise</a> its economy.<sup id="cite_ref-filbrn_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-filbrn-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Beginning in 1983, ethnic tensions were manifested in an <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Sri Lankan Civil War">on-and-off insurgency</a> against the government by the LTTE. An <a href="/wiki/Four_Four_Bravo" title="Four Four Bravo">LTTE attack on 13 soldiers</a> resulted in the start of a civil war, and in response anti-Tamil <a href="/wiki/Black_July" title="Black July">race riots</a> took place, allegedly backed by <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_Buddhist_nationalism" title="Sinhalese Buddhist nationalism">Sinhalese hard-line</a> ministers, which resulted in more than 150,000 Tamil civilians fleeing the island, seeking asylum in other countries.<sup id="cite_ref-Remembering_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Remembering-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-lkwbblk_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lkwbblk-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
