</p><p>Lapses in foreign policy resulted in India strengthening the Tigers by providing arms and training.<sup id="cite_ref-sndaytst_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sndaytst-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> In 1987, the <a href="/wiki/Indo-Sri_Lanka_Accord" title="Indo-Sri Lanka Accord">Indo-Sri Lanka Accord</a> was signed and the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force" title="Indian Peace Keeping Force">Indian Peace Keeping Force</a> (IPKF) was deployed in northern Sri Lanka to stabilise the region by neutralising the LTTE.<sup id="cite_ref-Stokke2000a_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stokke2000a-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The same year, the <a href="/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna" title="Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna">JVP</a> launched its <a href="/wiki/1987%E2%80%9389_JVP_Insurrection" class="mw-redirect" title="1987–89 JVP Insurrection">second insurrection</a> in Southern Sri Lanka,<sup id="cite_ref-secninsr_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-secninsr-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> necessitating redeployment of the IPKF in 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-atimes_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-atimes-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> In October 1990, the LTTE <a href="/wiki/Expulsion_of_Muslims_from_the_Northern_province_by_LTTE" class="mw-redirect" title="Expulsion of Muslims from the Northern province by LTTE">expelled Sri Lankan Moors</a> (Muslims by religion) from northern Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-slnp_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slnp-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> In 2002, the Sri Lankan government and LTTE signed a Norwegian-mediated ceasefire agreement.<sup id="cite_ref-a1_124-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-a1-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami" title="2004 Indian Ocean earthquake and tsunami">2004 Asian tsunami</a> killed over 30,000 and displaced over 500,000 people in Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_2005_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_2005-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-wsws1_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wsws1-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> From 1985 to 2006, the Sri Lankan government and Tamil insurgents held four rounds of peace talks without success. Both LTTE and the government resumed fighting in 2006, and the government officially backed out of the ceasefire in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-a1_124-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-a1-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, under the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Sri_Lanka" title="President of Sri Lanka">Presidency</a> of <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa" title="Mahinda Rajapaksa">Mahinda Rajapaksa</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces" title="Sri Lanka Armed Forces">Sri Lanka Armed Forces</a> defeated the LTTE, bringing an end to the civil war on 19 May 2009<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>, and re-established control of the entire country by the Sri Lankan Government.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> Overall, between 60,000 and 100,000 people were killed during the 26 years of conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-ABC200509_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABC200509-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/2019_Sri_Lanka_Easter_bombings" title="2019 Sri Lanka Easter bombings">2019 Sri Lanka Easter bombings</a> carried out by the terrorist group <a href="/wiki/National_Thowheeth_Jama%27ath" title="National Thowheeth Jama&#39;ath">National Thowheeth Jama'ath</a> on 21 April 2019 resulted in the death of 261 innocent people.<sup id="cite_ref-timeline_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-timeline-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> On 26 April 2019 an anti <a href="/wiki/Sainthamaruthu_shootout" title="Sainthamaruthu shootout">terrorist operation</a> was carried out against the National Thowheeth Jama'ath by the Sri Lankan army with the operation being successful and National Thowheeth Jama'ath's insurgency ending.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>  
</p><p>Economic troubles in Sri Lanka began in 2019, when a <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%93present_Sri_Lankan_economic_crisis" title="2019–present Sri Lankan economic crisis">severe economic crisis</a> occurred caused by rapidly increasing foreign debt, massive government budget deficits due to tax cuts, a food crisis caused by mandatory organic farming along with a ban on chemical fertilizers, and a multitude of other factors.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> The Sri Lankan Government officially declared the ongoing crisis to be the worst <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Economy of Sri Lanka">economic crisis in the country</a> in 73 years.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> In August 2021, a food emergency was declared.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> In June 2022, Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe" title="Ranil Wickremesinghe">Ranil Wickremesinghe</a> declared the collapse of the Sri Lankan economy in parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> The crisis resulted in Sri Lanka defaulting on its $51 billion sovereign debt for the first time in its history, along with double-digit inflation, a crippling energy crisis that led to 15 hour power cuts, severe fuel shortages leading to the suspension of fuel to all non-essential vehicles, and more.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Due to the crisis, massive <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests" title="2022 Sri Lankan protests">street protests</a> erupted across the country, with protesters demanding the resignation of the then-incumbent President <a href="/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa" title="Gotabaya Rajapaksa">Gotabaya Rajapaksa</a>. The protests culminated with the storming of the <a href="/wiki/President%27s_House,_Colombo" title="President&#39;s House, Colombo">President's House</a> on July 9, 2022, and resulted in President Gotabaya Rajapaksa fleeing to Singapore<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> and later email his resignation to parliament, formally announcing his resignation and making him the first Sri Lankan president to resign in the middle of his term.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> On the same day the President's House was stormed, protesters stormed the <a href="/wiki/115_Fifth_Lane" title="115 Fifth Lane">private residence of the prime minister</a> and burnt it down.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On July 20, 2022, <a href="/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe" title="Ranil Wickremesinghe">Ranil Wickremesinghe</a> was elected as the ninth President via a <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election" title="2022 Sri Lankan presidential election">parliamentarian election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Geography of Sri Lanka">Geography of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg" class="image"><img alt="A roughly oval island with a mountainous centre" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg/170px-Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="295" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg/255px-Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg/340px-Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="5200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Topography_Sri_Lanka.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Topographic map of Sri Lanka</div></div></div>
<p>Sri Lanka, an island in <a href="/wiki/South_Asia" title="South Asia">South Asia</a> shaped as a teardrop or a pear/<a href="/wiki/Mango" title="Mango">mango</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> lies on the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Plate" title="Indian Plate">Indian Plate</a>, a major <a href="/wiki/Plate_tectonics" title="Plate tectonics">tectonic plate</a> that was formerly part of the <a href="/wiki/Indo-Australian_Plate" title="Indo-Australian Plate">Indo-Australian Plate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> It is in the Indian Ocean southwest of the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Bengal" title="Bay of Bengal">Bay of Bengal</a>, between latitudes <a href="/wiki/5th_parallel_north" title="5th parallel north">5°</a> and <a href="/wiki/10th_parallel_north" title="10th parallel north">10°&#160;N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/79th_meridian_east" title="79th meridian east">79°</a> and <a href="/wiki/82nd_meridian_east" title="82nd meridian east">82°&#160;E</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka is separated from the mainland portion of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_subcontinent" title="Indian subcontinent">Indian subcontinent</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Mannar" title="Gulf of Mannar">Gulf of Mannar</a> and <a href="/wiki/Palk_Strait" title="Palk Strait">Palk Strait</a>. According to <a href="/wiki/Hindu_mythology" title="Hindu mythology">Hindu mythology</a>, a land bridge existed between the Indian mainland and Sri Lanka. It now amounts to only a <a href="/wiki/Adam%27s_Bridge" title="Adam&#39;s Bridge">chain of limestone shoals</a> remaining above <a href="/wiki/Sea_level" title="Sea level">sea level</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Legends claim that it was passable on foot up to 1480 CE, until <a href="/wiki/Cyclone" title="Cyclone">cyclones</a> deepened the channel.<sup id="cite_ref-Garg1992_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Garg1992-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> Portions are still as shallow as 1 metre (3&#160;ft), hindering navigation.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The island consists mostly of flat to rolling coastal plains, with mountains rising only in the south-central part. The highest point is <a href="/wiki/Pidurutalagala" title="Pidurutalagala">Pidurutalagala</a>, reaching 2,524 metres (8,281&#160;ft) above sea level.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Paada1.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Sri_Paada1.JPG/220px-Sri_Paada1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Sri_Paada1.JPG/330px-Sri_Paada1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Sri_Paada1.JPG/440px-Sri_Paada1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2012" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Paada1.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A view of <a href="/wiki/Adam%27s_Peak" title="Adam&#39;s Peak">Sripada</a> from <a href="/wiki/Maskeliya" title="Maskeliya">Maskeliya</a></div></div></div>
<p>Sri Lanka has 103 rivers. The longest of these is the <a href="/wiki/Mahaweli_River" title="Mahaweli River">Mahaweli River</a>, extending 335 kilometres (208&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> These waterways give rise to 51 natural waterfalls of 10 metres (33&#160;ft) or more. The highest is <a href="/wiki/Bambarakanda_Falls" title="Bambarakanda Falls">Bambarakanda Falls</a>, with a height of 263 metres (863&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka's coastline is 1,585&#160;km (985&#160;mi) long.<sup id="cite_ref-unep_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unep-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka claims an <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> extending 200 <a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nautical miles</a>, which is approximately 6.7 times Sri Lanka's land area. The coastline and adjacent waters support highly productive marine ecosystems such as <a href="/wiki/Fringing_reef" title="Fringing reef">fringing</a> <a href="/wiki/Coral_reef" title="Coral reef">coral reefs</a> and shallow beds of coastal and <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuarine</a> <a href="/wiki/Seagrass" title="Seagrass">seagrasses</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-faosl_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-faosl-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka has 45 <a href="/wiki/Estuary" title="Estuary">estuaries</a> and 40 <a href="/wiki/Lagoon" title="Lagoon">lagoons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-unep_174-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unep-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka's <a href="/wiki/Mangrove" title="Mangrove">mangrove</a> ecosystem spans over 7,000 <a href="/wiki/Hectare" title="Hectare">hectares</a> and played a vital role in buffering the force of the waves in the <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami" title="2004 Indian Ocean earthquake and tsunami">2004 Indian Ocean tsunami</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-iucnas_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iucnas-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> The island is rich in minerals such as <a href="/wiki/Ilmenite" title="Ilmenite">ilmenite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Feldspar" title="Feldspar">feldspar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Graphite" title="Graphite">graphite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Silica" class="mw-redirect" title="Silica">silica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kaolin" class="mw-redirect" title="Kaolin">kaolin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mica" title="Mica">mica</a> and <a href="/wiki/Thorium" title="Thorium">thorium</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-minr_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-minr-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-thor_178-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thor-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> Existence of <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a> and gas in the Gulf of Mannar has also been confirmed, and the extraction of recoverable quantities is underway.<sup id="cite_ref-prdssl_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-prdssl-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Forestry_in_Sri_Lanka" class="mw-redirect" title="Forestry in Sri Lanka">Forestry in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg/220px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg/330px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg/440px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1208" data-file-height="850" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_LKA_present.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sri Lanka map of Köppen climate classification</div></div></div>
<p>The climate is <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical</a> and warm because of moderating effects of ocean winds. Mean temperatures range from 17&#160;°C (62.6&#160;°F) in the <a href="/wiki/Central_Highlands_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Central Highlands of Sri Lanka">central highlands</a>, where frost may occur for several days in the winter, to a maximum of 33&#160;°C (91.4&#160;°F) in low-altitude areas. Average yearly temperatures range from 28&#160;°C (82.4&#160;°F) to nearly 31&#160;°C (87.8&#160;°F). Day and night temperatures may vary by 14&#160;°C (57.2&#160;°F) to 18&#160;°C (64.4&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The rainfall pattern is influenced by <a href="/wiki/Monsoon" title="Monsoon">monsoon</a> winds from the Indian Ocean and Bay of Bengal. The "wet zone" and some of the windward slopes of the central highlands receive up to 2,500 millimetres (98.4&#160;in) of rain each year, but the leeward slopes in the east and northeast receive little rain. Most of the east, southeast, and northern parts of Sri Lanka constitute the "dry zone", which receives between 1,200 and 1,900&#160;mm (47 and 75&#160;in) of rain annually.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The arid northwest and southeast coasts receive the least rain at 800 to 1,200&#160;mm (31 to 47&#160;in) per year. Periodic squalls occur and sometimes <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">tropical cyclones</a> bring overcast skies and rains to the southwest, northeast, and eastern parts of the island. Humidity is typically higher in the southwest and mountainous areas and depends on the seasonal patterns of rainfall.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> An increase in average rainfall coupled with heavier rainfall events has resulted in recurrent flooding and related damages to infrastructure, utility supply and the urban economy.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Environment_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Environment of Sri Lanka">Environment of Sri Lanka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Wildlife of Sri Lanka">Wildlife of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Sri_Lanka" title="List of mammals of Sri Lanka">List of mammals of Sri Lanka</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Sri_Lanka" title="List of birds of Sri Lanka">List of birds of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg/220px-Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg/330px-Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg/440px-Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Elephas_maximus_maximus_-_01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_elephant" title="Sri Lankan elephant">Sri Lankan elephant</a> is one of three recognised <a href="/wiki/Subspecies" title="Subspecies">subspecies</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Asian_elephant" title="Asian elephant">Asian elephant</a>. The 2011 elephant census estimated a population of 5,879.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Western_Ghats" title="Western Ghats">Western Ghats</a> of India and Sri Lanka were included among the first 18 global <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot" title="Biodiversity hotspot">biodiversity hotspots</a> due to high levels of species endemism. The number of biodiversity hotspots has now increased to 34.<sup id="cite_ref-Current_Science_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Current_Science-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka has the highest biodiversity per unit area among Asian countries for flowering plants and all vertebrate groups except birds.<sup id="cite_ref-mahindapala_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mahindapala-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> A remarkably high proportion of the species among its flora and fauna, 27% of the 3,210 flowering plants and 22% of the mammals, are <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka supports a rich avifauna of that stands at 453 species and this include 240 species of birds that are known to breed in the country. <a href="/wiki/List_of_endemic_birds_of_Sri_Lanka" title="List of endemic birds of Sri Lanka">33 species</a> are accepted by some ornithologists as endemic while some ornithologists consider only 27 are endemic and the remaining six are considered as proposed endemics.<sup id="cite_ref-redlist2012_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-redlist2012-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka's protected areas are administrated by two government bodies; The <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Forest_Conservation_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Department of Forest Conservation (Sri Lanka)">Department of Forest Conservation</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Wildlife_Conservation_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Department of Wildlife Conservation (Sri Lanka)">Department of Wildlife Conservation</a>. Department of Wildlife Conservation administrates 61 wildlife sanctuaries, 22 national parks, four nature reserves, three strict nature reserves, and one jungle corridor while Department of Forest Conservation oversees 65 conservation forests and one national heritage wilderness area. 26.5% of the country's land area is legally protected. This is a higher percentage of protected areas when compared to the rest of Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka contains four terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_lowland_rain_forests" title="Sri Lanka lowland rain forests">Sri Lanka lowland rain forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_montane_rain_forests" title="Sri Lanka montane rain forests">Sri Lanka montane rain forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_dry-zone_dry_evergreen_forests" title="Sri Lanka dry-zone dry evergreen forests">Sri Lanka dry-zone dry evergreen forests</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Deccan_thorn_scrub_forests" title="Deccan thorn scrub forests">Deccan thorn scrub forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> Flowering <a href="/wiki/Acacia" title="Acacia">acacias</a> flourish on the arid <a href="/wiki/Jaffna_Peninsula" title="Jaffna Peninsula">Jaffna Peninsula</a>. Among the trees of the dry-land forests are valuable species such as <a href="/wiki/Chloroxylon" title="Chloroxylon">satinwood</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ebony" title="Ebony">ebony</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mesua_ferrea" title="Mesua ferrea">ironwood</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mahogany" title="Mahogany">mahogany</a> and <a href="/wiki/Teak" title="Teak">teak</a>. The wet zone is a tropical evergreen forest with tall trees, broad foliage, and a dense undergrowth of vines and creepers. Subtropical evergreen forests resembling those of temperate climates flourish in the higher altitudes.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rainbirder_-_Leopard_(1).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg/220px-Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg/330px-Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg/440px-Rainbirder_-_Leopard_%281%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="676" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rainbirder_-_Leopard_(1).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_leopard" title="Sri Lankan leopard">Sri Lankan leopard</a> (<i>Panthera pardus kotiya</i>) is an <a href="/wiki/Endangered_species" title="Endangered species">endangered</a> subspecies of <a href="/wiki/Leopard" title="Leopard">leopard</a> native to Sri Lanka.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Yala_National_Park" title="Yala National Park">Yala National Park</a> in the southeast protects herds of elephant, deer, and peacocks. The <a href="/wiki/Wilpattu_National_Park" title="Wilpattu National Park">Wilpattu National Park</a> in the northwest, the largest national park, preserves the habitats of many water birds such as storks, pelicans, ibis, and spoonbills. The island has four <a href="/wiki/Nature_reserve" title="Nature reserve">biosphere reserves</a>: <a href="/wiki/Bundala_National_Park" title="Bundala National Park">Bundala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hurulu_Forest_Reserve" title="Hurulu Forest Reserve">Hurulu Forest Reserve</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kanneliya-Dediyagala-Nakiyadeniya" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanneliya-Dediyagala-Nakiyadeniya">Kanneliya-Dediyagala-Nakiyadeniya</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sinharaja_Forest_Reserve" title="Sinharaja Forest Reserve">Sinharaja</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-unesco_biosphere_reserves_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unesco_biosphere_reserves-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Sinharaja is home to 26 endemic birds and 20 rainforest species, including the elusive <a href="/wiki/Red-faced_malkoha" title="Red-faced malkoha">red-faced malkoha</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Green-billed_coucal" title="Green-billed coucal">green-billed coucal</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_blue_magpie" title="Sri Lanka blue magpie">Sri Lanka blue magpie</a>. The untapped genetic potential of Sinharaja flora is enormous. Of the 211 woody trees and lianas within the reserve, 139 (66%) are endemic. The total vegetation density, including trees, shrubs, herbs, and seedlings, has been estimated at 240,000 individuals per hectare. The Minneriya National Park borders the <a href="/wiki/Minneriya_Tank" title="Minneriya Tank">Minneriya Tank</a>, which is an important source of water for elephants inhabiting the surrounding forests. Dubbed "The Gathering", the congregation of elephants can be seen on the tank-bed in the late dry season (August to October) as the surrounding water sources steadily disappear. The park also encompasses a range of micro-habitats which include classic dry zone tropical monsoonal evergreen forest, thick stands of giant bamboo, hilly pastures (patanas), and grasslands (talawas).<sup id="cite_ref-Minneriya_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Minneriya-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg/220px-Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg/330px-Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg/440px-Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1356" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rhododendron_arboreum_zeylanicum_flower.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Maha rath mala (<i>Rhododendron arboreum</i> ssp. <i>zeylanicum</i>) is a rare sub-species of <i><a href="/wiki/Rhododendron_arboreum" title="Rhododendron arboreum">Rhododendron arboreum</a></i> found in <a href="/wiki/Central_Highlands_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Central Highlands of Sri Lanka">Central Highlands of Sri Lanka</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>During the Mahaweli Program of the 1970s and 1980s in northern Sri Lanka, the government set aside four areas of land totalling 1,900&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (730&#160;sq&#160;mi) as national parks. Statistics of Sri Lanka's forest cover show rapid deforestation from 1956 to 2010. In 1956, 44.2 percent of the country's land area had forest cover. Forest cover depleted rapidly in recent decades; 29.6 percent in 1999, 28.7 percent in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-forests_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-forests-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Constitution of Sri Lanka">Constitution of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Old_Parliament_Building,_Colombo.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG/220px-Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG/330px-Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG/440px-Old_Parliament_Building%2C_Colombo.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4965" data-file-height="3310" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Old_Parliament_Building,_Colombo.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Old_Parliament_Building,_Colombo" title="Old Parliament Building, Colombo">old Sri Lankan Parliament building</a>, near the <a href="/wiki/Galle_Face_Green" title="Galle Face Green">Galle Face Green</a> in Colombo. It now serves as the <a href="/wiki/Presidential_Secretariat_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Presidential Secretariat (Sri Lanka)">Presidential Secretariat</a>'s headquarters.</div></div></div>
<p>Sri Lanka is a <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary state</a> which is governed by a <a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Semi-presidential system">semi-presidential system</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-constnt_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constnt-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka is the oldest democracy in Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Most provisions of the constitution can be amended by a <a href="/wiki/Supermajority" title="Supermajority">two-thirds majority</a> in <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Parliament of Sri Lanka">parliament</a>. The amendment of <a href="/wiki/Entrenched_clause" title="Entrenched clause">certain basic features</a> such as the clauses on language, religion, and reference to Sri Lanka as a unitary state require both a two-thirds majority and approval in a nationwide <a href="/wiki/Referendum" title="Referendum">referendum</a>.
</p><p>In common with many democracies, the Sri Lankan government has three branches:
</p>
<ul><li><b>Executive</b>: The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Sri_Lanka" title="President of Sri Lanka">President of Sri Lanka</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander in chief</a> of the armed forces; <a href="/wiki/Chief_executive_(gubernatorial)" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief executive (gubernatorial)">chief executive</a>, and is popularly elected for a five-year term.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> The president heads the <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_(government)" title="Cabinet (government)">cabinet</a> and appoints <a href="/wiki/Minister_(government)" title="Minister (government)">ministers</a> from elected <a href="/wiki/Member_of_parliament" title="Member of parliament">members of parliament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> The president is immune from legal proceedings while in the office with respect to any acts done or omitted to be done by him or her in either an official or private capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Following the passage of the <a href="/wiki/Nineteenth_Amendment_to_the_Constitution_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Nineteenth Amendment to the Constitution of Sri Lanka">19th amendment to the constitution</a> in 2015, the president has two terms, which previously stood at no term limit.</li>
<li><b>Legislative</b>: The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Parliament of Sri Lanka">Parliament of Sri Lanka</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral</a> 225-member legislature with 196 members elected in multi-seat constituencies and 29 elected by <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Members are elected by universal suffrage for a five-year term. The president may summon, suspend, or end a legislative session and dissolve Parliament at any time after four and a half years. The parliament reserves the power to make all laws.<sup id="cite_ref-parliam_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parliam-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> The president's deputy and <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Prime Minister of Sri Lanka">prime minister</a>, leads the <a href="/wiki/Ruling_party" title="Ruling party">ruling party</a> in parliament and shares many executive responsibilities, mainly in domestic affairs.</li></ul>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg/220px-Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg/330px-Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg/440px-Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="399" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Supreme_Court_Colombo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Supreme Court of Sri Lanka, Colombo</div></div></div>
<ul><li><b>Judicial</b>: Sri Lanka's judiciary consists of a <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Supreme Court of Sri Lanka">Supreme Court</a> – the highest and final superior court of record,<sup id="cite_ref-parliam_201-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parliam-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> a Court of Appeal, High Courts and a number of subordinate courts. The highly complex legal system reflects diverse cultural influences.<sup id="cite_ref-unpanh_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unpanh-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> Criminal law is based almost entirely on <a href="/wiki/English_law" title="English law">British law</a>. Basic civil law derives from <a href="/wiki/Roman-Dutch_law" title="Roman-Dutch law">Roman-Dutch law</a>. Laws pertaining to marriage, divorce, and inheritance are <a href="/wiki/Common_law" title="Common law">communal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-lawsd_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lawsd-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Because of ancient customary practices and religion, the Sinhala customary law (Kandyan law), the <a href="/wiki/Thesavalamai" title="Thesavalamai">Thesavalamai</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sharia" title="Sharia">Sharia</a> law are followed in special cases.<sup id="cite_ref-custmryl_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-custmryl-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> The president appoints judges to the Supreme Court, the Court of Appeal, and the High Courts. A judicial service commission, composed of the <a href="/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Chief Justice of Sri Lanka">chief justice</a> and two Supreme Court judges, appoints, transfers, and dismisses lower court judges.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Politics of Sri Lanka">Politics of Sri Lanka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Elections in Sri Lanka">Elections in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="background:#EEEEEE; font-size: 100%; white-space: nowrap;"><span class="flagicon"><a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka"><img alt="Sri Lanka" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/23px-Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png" decoding="async" width="23" height="12" class="thumbborder" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/35px-Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/46px-Flag_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="600" /></a></span> <a href="/wiki/National_symbols_of_Sri_Lanka" title="National symbols of Sri Lanka">National symbols of Sri Lanka</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Flag</th><td macroura">Grizzled giant squirrel</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Bird</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_junglefowl" class="mw-redirect" title="Sri Lanka class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"/><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Sri_Lankan_symbols" title="Template:Sri Lankan symbols"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Sri_Lankan_symbols" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Sri_Lankan_symbols&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The current political culture in Sri Lanka is a contest between two rival coalitions led by the <a href="/wiki/Centre-left_politics" title="Centre-left politics">centre-left</a> and <a href="/wiki/Progressivism" title="Progressivism">progressive</a> <a href="/wiki/United_People%27s_Freedom_Alliance" title="United People&#39;s Freedom Alliance">United People's Freedom Alliance</a> (UPFA), an offspring of <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Freedom_Party" title="Sri Lanka Freedom Party">Sri Lanka Freedom Party</a> (SLFP), and the comparatively <a href="/wiki/Right-wing_politics" title="Right-wing politics">right-wing</a> and pro-<a href="/wiki/Capitalism" title="Capitalism">capitalist</a> <a href="/wiki/United_National_Party" title="United National Party">United National Party</a> (UNP).<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka is essentially a multi-party democracy with many smaller Buddhist, socialist, and Tamil nationalist political parties. As of July 2011, the number of registered political parties in the country is 67.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> Of these, the <a href="/wiki/Lanka_Sama_Samaja_Party" title="Lanka Sama Samaja Party">Lanka Sama Samaja Party</a> (LSSP), established in 1935, is the oldest.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The UNP, established by D. S. Senanayake in 1946, was until recently the largest single political party.<sup id="cite_ref-unpl_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unpl-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> It is the only political group which had representation in all parliaments since independence.<sup id="cite_ref-unpl_210-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unpl-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> SLFP was founded by <a href="/wiki/S._W._R._D._Bandaranaike" title="S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike">S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike</a> in July 1951.<sup id="cite_ref-slfps_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slfps-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> SLFP registered its first victory in 1956, defeating the ruling UNP in the <a href="/wiki/1956_Ceylonese_parliamentary_election" title="1956 Ceylonese parliamentary election">1956 Parliamentary election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-slfps_211-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slfps-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Following the parliamentary election in <a href="/wiki/July_1960_Ceylonese_parliamentary_election" title="July 1960 Ceylonese parliamentary election">July 1960</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sirimavo_Bandaranaike" title="Sirimavo Bandaranaike">Sirimavo Bandaranaike</a> became the prime minister and the world's first elected <a href="/wiki/List_of_elected_and_appointed_female_heads_of_state_and_government" title="List of elected and appointed female heads of state and government">female head of government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbcsirimavo_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbcsirimavo-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/G._G._Ponnambalam" title="G. G. Ponnambalam">G. G. Ponnambalam</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Tamil_nationalism" title="Sri Lankan Tamil nationalism">Tamil nationalist</a> counterpart of S. W. R. D. Bandaranaike,<sup id="cite_ref-socs_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-socs-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> founded the <a href="/wiki/All_Ceylon_Tamil_Congress" title="All Ceylon Tamil Congress">All Ceylon Tamil Congress</a> (ACTC) in 1944. Objecting to Ponnambalam's cooperation with D. S. Senanayake, a dissident group led by <a href="/wiki/S._J._V._Chelvanayakam" title="S. J. V. Chelvanayakam">S.J.V. Chelvanayakam</a> broke away in 1949 and formed the <a href="/wiki/Illankai_Tamil_Arasu_Kachchi" class="mw-redirect" title="Illankai Tamil Arasu Kachchi">Illankai Tamil Arasu Kachchi</a> (ITAK), also known as the Federal Party, becoming the main Tamil political party in Sri Lanka for next two decades.<sup id="cite_ref-poltitak_214-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-poltitak-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> The Federal Party advocated a more aggressive stance toward the Sinhalese.<sup id="cite_ref-postcolhist_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-postcolhist-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> With the constitutional reforms of 1972, the ACTC and ITAK created the Tamil United Front (later <a href="/wiki/Tamil_United_Liberation_Front" title="Tamil United Liberation Front">Tamil United Liberation Front</a>). Following a period of turbulence as Tamil militants rose to power in the late 1970s, these Tamil political parties were succeeded in October 2001 by the <a href="/wiki/Tamil_National_Alliance" title="Tamil National Alliance">Tamil National Alliance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-postcolhist_215-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-postcolhist-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tnas_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tnas-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna" title="Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna">Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism" title="Marxism–Leninism">Marxist–Leninist</a> political party founded by <a href="/wiki/Rohana_Wijeweera" title="Rohana Wijeweera">Rohana Wijeweera</a> in 1965, serves as a third force in the current political context.<sup id="cite_ref-jvpnew_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jvpnew-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> It endorses leftist policies which are more radical than the traditionalist leftist politics of the LSSP and the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Communist Party of Sri Lanka">Communist Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-postcolhist_215-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-postcolhist-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Founded in 1981, the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Muslim_Congress" title="Sri Lanka Muslim Congress">Sri Lanka Muslim Congress</a> is the largest Muslim political party in Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-muslcon_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-muslcon-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>President <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_Rajapaksa" title="Mahinda Rajapaksa">Mahinda Rajapaksa</a> lost the <a href="/wiki/2015_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election" title="2015 Sri Lankan presidential election">2015 presidential elections</a>, ending his ten-year presidency. However, his successor as Sri Lankan President, <a href="/wiki/Maithripala_Sirisena" title="Maithripala Sirisena">Maithripala Sirisena</a>, decided not to seek re-election in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> The Rajapaksa family regained power in November <a href="/wiki/2019_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election" title="2019 Sri Lankan presidential election">2019 presidential elections</a> when Mahinda's younger brother and former wartime defence chief <a href="/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa" title="Gotabaya Rajapaksa">Gotabaya Rajapaksa</a> won the election, and he was later sworn in as the new president of Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Their firm grip of power was consolidated in the <a href="/wiki/2020_Sri_Lankan_parliamentary_election" title="2020 Sri Lankan parliamentary election">parliamentary elections</a> in August 2020. The family's political party, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_People%27s_Front" class="mw-redirect" title="Sri Lanka People&#39;s Front">Sri Lanka People's Front</a> (known by its Sinhala initials SLPP), obtained a landslide victory and a clear majority in the parliament. Five members of the Rajapaksa family won seats in the new parliament. Former president Mahinda Rajapaksa became the new prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2022, a <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_political_crisis" title="2022 Sri Lankan political crisis">political crisis</a> started due to the power struggle between <a href="/wiki/President_of_Sri_Lanka" title="President of Sri Lanka">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Gotabaya_Rajapaksa" title="Gotabaya Rajapaksa">Gotabaya Rajapaksa</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Parliament of Sri Lanka">Parliament of Sri Lanka</a>. The crisis was fuelled by <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_protests" title="2022 Sri Lankan protests">anti-government protests and demonstrations by the public</a> and also due to the worsening <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Economy of Sri Lanka">economy of Sri Lanka</a> since 2019. The anti-government sentiment across various parts of Sri Lanka has triggered unprecedented political instability, creating shockwaves in the political arena.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On July 20, 2022, <a href="/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe" title="Ranil Wickremesinghe">Ranil Wickremesinghe</a> was elected as the ninth President via a <a href="/wiki/2022_Sri_Lankan_presidential_election" title="2022 Sri Lankan presidential election">parliamentarian election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Administrative divisions of Sri Lanka">Administrative divisions of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>For administrative purposes, Sri Lanka is divided into nine <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Provinces of Sri Lanka">provinces</a><sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> and twenty-five <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Districts of Sri Lanka">districts</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Provinces">Provinces</span></h3>
<p>Provinces in Sri Lanka have existed since the 19th century, but they had no legal status until 1987 when the 13th Amendment of the 1978 constitution established provincial councils after several decades of increasing demand for a <a href="/wiki/Devolution" title="Devolution">decentralisation</a> of the government.<sup id="cite_ref-official_227-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> Each provincial council is an autonomous body not under the authority of any ministry. Some of its functions had been undertaken by central government ministries, departments, corporations, and statutory authorities,<sup id="cite_ref-official_227-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> but authority over land and police is not as a rule given to provincial councils.<sup id="cite_ref-landpolc_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-landpolc-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-landpolc2_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-landpolc2-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> Between 1989 and 2006, the Northern and Eastern provinces were temporarily merged to form the <a href="/wiki/North_Eastern_Province,_Sri_Lanka" title="North Eastern Province, Sri Lanka">North-East Province</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-lnpx_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lnpx-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-norteastn_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-norteastn-231">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> Prior to 1987, all administrative tasks for the provinces were handled by a district-based civil service which had been in place since colonial times. Now each province is administered by a directly elected provincial council:
</p>
<div role="img" class="floatleft noresize" style="width: 400px; line-height: 1; text-align: center; background-color: #ffffff; position: relative;"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Sri_Lankan_Provinces_and_districts.PNG/400px-Sri_Lankan_Provinces_and_districts.PNG" decoding="async" width="400" height="639" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Sri_Lankan_Provinces_and_districts.PNG/600px-Sri_Lankan_Provinces_and_districts.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/57/Sri_Lankan_Provinces_and_districts.PNG 2x" data-file-width="601" data-file-height="960" />
