<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Districts_and_local_authorities">Districts and local authorities</span></h3>
<p>Each district is administered under a <a href="/wiki/Government_Agent_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Government Agent (Sri Lanka)">district secretariat</a>. The districts are further subdivided into 256 <a href="/wiki/Divisional_Secretariats_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Divisional Secretariats of Sri Lanka">divisional secretariats</a>, and these to approximately 14,008 <a href="/wiki/Grama_Niladhari" title="Grama Niladhari">Grama Niladhari</a> divisions.<sup id="cite_ref-tstasgov_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tstasgov-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> The districts are known in Sinhala as <i>disa</i> and in Tamil as <i>māwaddam</i>. Originally, a <i>disa</i> (usually rendered into English as Dissavony) was a <a href="/wiki/Duchy" title="Duchy">duchy</a>, notably Matale and Uva.
</p><p>There are three other types of local authorities: municipal councils (18), urban councils (13) and pradeshiya sabha, also called pradesha sabhai (256).<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> Local authorities were originally based on feudal counties named <i>korale</i> and <i>rata</i>, and were formerly known as "D.R.O. divisions" after the divisional revenue officer.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> Later, the D.R.O.s became "assistant government agents," and the divisions were known as "A.G.A. divisions". These divisional secretariats are currently administered by a divisional secretary.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Foreign relations of Sri Lanka">Foreign relations of Sri Lanka</a></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_and_the_Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Sri Lanka and the Non-Aligned Movement">Sri Lanka and the Non-Aligned Movement</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg/220px-Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg/330px-Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg/440px-Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="750" data-file-height="499" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jayewardene_presents_elephant_to_Reagan.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>President <a href="/wiki/J._R._Jayewardene" title="J. R. Jayewardene">J. R. Jayewardene</a> gifting a baby elephant to US President <a href="/wiki/Ronald_Reagan" title="Ronald Reagan">Ronald Reagan</a> in 1984</div></div></div>
<p>Sri Lanka is a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a> (NAM). While ensuring that it maintains its independence, Sri Lanka has cultivated <a href="/wiki/India%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations" title="India–Sri Lanka relations">relations with India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-relatns_238-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-relatns-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka became a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> in 1955. Today, it is also a member of the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a>, the <a href="/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation" title="South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation">SAARC</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Asian_Development_Bank" title="Asian Development Bank">Asian Development Bank</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Colombo_Plan" title="Colombo Plan">Colombo Plan</a>.
</p><p>The United National Party has traditionally favoured links with the West, while the Sri Lanka Freedom Party has favoured links with the East.<sup id="cite_ref-relatns_238-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-relatns-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lankan Finance Minister J. R. Jayewardene, together with then Australian Foreign Minister Sir Percy Spencer, proposed the Colombo Plan at the Commonwealth Foreign Minister's Conference held in Colombo in 1950.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> At the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_San_Francisco" title="Treaty of San Francisco">San Francisco Peace Conference</a> in 1951, while many countries were reluctant, Sri Lanka argued for a free <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a> and refused to accept payment of reparations for <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a> damage because it believed it would harm Japan's economy.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka-China relations started as soon as the People's Republic of China was formed in 1949. The two countries signed an important Rice-Rubber Pact in 1952.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka played a vital role at the <a href="/wiki/Asian%E2%80%93African_Conference" class="mw-redirect" title="Asian–African Conference">Asian–African Conference</a> in 1955, which was an important step in the crystallisation of the NAM.<sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Bandaranaike government of 1956 significantly changed the pro-western policies set by the previous UNP government. It recognised Cuba under <a href="/wiki/Fidel_Castro" title="Fidel Castro">Fidel Castro</a> in 1959. Shortly afterward, Cuba's revolutionary <a href="/wiki/Che_Guevara" title="Che Guevara">Che Guevara</a> paid a visit to Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Sirima%E2%80%93Shastri_Pact" title="Sirima–Shastri Pact">Sirima-Shastri Pact</a> of 1964<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Sirima%E2%80%93Gandhi_Pact" title="Sirima–Gandhi Pact">Sirima-Gandhi Pact</a> of 1974<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> were signed between Sri Lankan and Indian leaders in an attempt to solve the <a href="/wiki/Ceylon_Citizenship_Act" title="Ceylon Citizenship Act">long-standing dispute</a> over the status of <a href="/wiki/Indian_Tamils_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Indian Tamils of Sri Lanka">plantation workers of Indian origin</a>. In 1974, <a href="/wiki/Katchatheevu" title="Katchatheevu">Kachchatheevu</a>, a small island in <a href="/wiki/Palk_Strait" title="Palk Strait">Palk Strait</a>, was formally ceded to Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> By this time, Sri Lanka was strongly involved in the NAM, and the <a href="/wiki/5th_Summit_of_the_Non-Aligned_Movement" title="5th Summit of the Non-Aligned Movement">fifth NAM summit</a> was held in Colombo in 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> The relationship between Sri Lanka and India became tense under the government of <a href="/wiki/Junius_Richard_Jayewardene" class="mw-redirect" title="Junius Richard Jayewardene">J. R. Jayawardene</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-atimes_143-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-atimes-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-indnrel_248-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-indnrel-248">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> As a result, <a href="/wiki/Indian_intervention_in_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" title="Indian intervention in the Sri Lankan Civil War">India intervened in the Sri Lankan Civil War</a> and subsequently deployed an <a href="/wiki/Indian_Peace_Keeping_Force" title="Indian Peace Keeping Force">Indian Peace Keeping Force</a> in 1987.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> In the present, Sri Lanka enjoys extensive relations with China,<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> Russia,<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> and Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces" title="Sri Lanka Armed Forces">Sri Lanka Armed Forces</a>, comprising the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Army" title="Sri Lanka Army">Sri Lanka Army</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Navy" title="Sri Lanka Navy">Sri Lanka Navy</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Air_Force" title="Sri Lanka Air Force">Sri Lanka Air Force</a>, come under the purview of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Ministry of Defence (Sri Lanka)">Ministry of Defence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> The total strength of the three services is around 346,000 personnel, with nearly 36,000 reserves.<sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka has not enforced <a href="/wiki/Conscription" title="Conscription">military conscription</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> Paramilitary units include the <a href="/wiki/Special_Task_Force_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Special Task Force (Sri Lanka)">Special Task Force</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Civil_Security_Force" title="Sri Lanka Civil Security Force">Civil Security Force</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Coast_Guard" title="Sri Lanka Coast Guard">Sri Lanka Coast Guard</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since independence in 1948, the primary focus of the armed forces has been internal security, crushing three major insurgencies, two by <a href="/wiki/Marxism" title="Marxism">Marxist</a> militants of the <a href="/wiki/Janatha_Vimukthi_Peramuna" title="Janatha Vimukthi Peramuna">JVP</a> and a 26-year-long conflict with the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam" title="Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam">LTTE</a>. The armed forces have been in a continuous mobilised state for the last 30 years.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup> The Sri Lankan Armed Forces have engaged in <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> <a href="/wiki/Peacekeeping" title="Peacekeeping">peacekeeping</a> operations since the early 1960s, contributing forces to permanent contingents deployed in several UN peacekeeping missions in <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Mission_in_the_Central_African_Republic_and_Chad" title="United Nations Mission in the Central African Republic and Chad">Chad</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Interim_Force_in_Lebanon" title="United Nations Interim Force in Lebanon">Lebanon</a>, and <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Stabilisation_Mission_in_Haiti" title="United Nations Stabilisation Mission in Haiti">Haiti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-unmissions09_260-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unmissions09-260">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Economy of Sri Lanka">Economy of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Agriculture in Sri Lanka">Agriculture in Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Tea production in Sri Lanka">Tea production in Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Tourism in Sri Lanka">Tourism in Sri Lanka</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Transport in Sri Lanka">Transport in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Sri_Lanka.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Development of real GDP per capita, 1820 to 2018</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Sri Lanka exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>According to the International Monetary Fund, Sri Lanka's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a> in terms of <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parity</a> is the second highest in the South Asian region in terms of <a href="/wiki/Per_capita_income" title="Per capita income">per capita income</a>. In the 19th and 20th centuries, Sri Lanka became a <a href="/wiki/Plantation_economy" title="Plantation economy">plantation economy</a> famous for its production and export of <a href="/wiki/Cinnamon" title="Cinnamon">cinnamon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rubber" class="mw-redirect" title="Rubber">rubber</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tea_production_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Tea production in Sri Lanka">Ceylon tea</a>, which remains a trademark national export.<sup id="cite_ref-plnyn_261-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-plnyn-261">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Ports_and_Aviation_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Ministry of Ports and Aviation (Sri Lanka)">development of modern ports</a> under British rule raised the strategic importance of the island as a centre of trade.<sup id="cite_ref-startim_262-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-startim-262">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup> From 1948 to 1977, socialism strongly influenced the government's economic policies. Colonial plantations were dismantled, industries were nationalised, and a <a href="/wiki/Welfare_state" title="Welfare state">welfare state</a> established. In 1977, the <a href="/wiki/Free_market" title="Free market">free market</a> economy was introduced to the country, incorporating privatisation, deregulation, and the promotion of private enterprise.<sup id="cite_ref-filbrn_135-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-filbrn-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg/220px-LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg/330px-LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg/440px-LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4928" data-file-height="3264" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:LK-Colombo-altes-parlament.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Colombo" title="World Trade Center Colombo">World Trade Center</a> in Colombo. <a href="/wiki/Presidential_Secretariat_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Presidential Secretariat (Sri Lanka)">Presidential Secretariat</a>, Bank of Ceylon and Galadari Hotel are also visible in the image.</div></div></div>
<p>While the production and export of tea, rubber, coffee, sugar, and other commodities remain important, industrialisation has increased the importance of food processing, textiles, telecommunications, and finance. The country's main economic sectors are tourism, tea export, clothing, rice production, and other agricultural products. In addition to these economic sectors, overseas employment, especially in the Middle East, contributes substantially in foreign exchange.<sup id="cite_ref-treasry_263-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treasry-263">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sri_Lanka&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the service sector makes up 59.7% of GDP, the industrial sector 26.2%, and the agriculture sector 8.4%.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> The private sector accounts for 85% of the economy.<sup id="cite_ref-adbrep_265-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adbrep-265">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> China, India and the United States are Sri Lanka's largest trading partners.<sup id="cite_ref-Sri_Lanka&#39;s_Trading_Partners_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sri_Lanka&#39;s_Trading_Partners-266">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> Economic disparities exist between the provinces with the Western Province contributing 45.1% of the GDP and the Southern Province and the Central Province contributing 10.7% and 10%, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-ecdisp_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ecdisp-267">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> With the end of the war, the Northern Province reported a record 22.9% GDP growth in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-northpr_268-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-northpr-268">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_030.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Sri_Lanka_030.jpg/220px-Sri_Lanka_030.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Sri_Lanka_030.jpg/330px-Sri_Lanka_030.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Sri_Lanka_030.jpg/440px-Sri_Lanka_030.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1723" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_030.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sri Lanka's most widely known export, <a href="/wiki/Ceylon_tea" title="Ceylon tea">Ceylon tea</a>, which <a href="/wiki/ISO" class="mw-redirect" title="ISO">ISO</a> considers the cleanest tea in the world in terms of pesticide residues. Sri Lanka is also the world's 2nd largest exporter of tea.<sup id="cite_ref-clnst_269-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-clnst-269">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The per capita income of Sri Lanka doubled from 2005 to 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-toplsl_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-toplsl-270">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup> During the same period, poverty dropped from 15.2% to 7.6%, unemployment rate dropped from 7.2% to 4.9%, <a href="/wiki/Market_capitalisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Market capitalisation">market capitalisation</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Colombo_Stock_Exchange" title="Colombo Stock Exchange">Colombo Stock Exchange</a> quadrupled, and the <a href="/wiki/Government_budget_deficit" class="mw-redirect" title="Government budget deficit">budget deficit</a> doubled.<sup id="cite_ref-treasry_263-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treasry-263">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> 99% of the households in Sri Lanka are electrified; 93.2% of the population have access to safe drinking water; and 53.1% have access to pipe-borne water.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> Income inequality has also dropped in recent years, indicated by a <a href="/wiki/Gini_coefficient" title="Gini coefficient">Gini coefficient</a> of 0.36 in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-statgovineq_271-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statgovineq-271">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2011 <a href="/wiki/Global_Competitiveness_Report" title="Global Competitiveness Report">Global Competitiveness Report</a>, published by the <a href="/wiki/World_Economic_Forum" title="World Economic Forum">World Economic Forum</a>, described Sri Lanka's economy as transitioning from the factor-driven stage to the efficiency-driven stage and that it ranked 52nd in global competitiveness.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> Also, out of the 142 countries surveyed, Sri Lanka ranked 45th in health and primary education, 32nd in business sophistication, 42nd in innovation, and 41st in goods market efficiency. In 2016, Sri Lanka ranked 5th in the <a href="/wiki/World_Giving_Index" title="World Giving Index">World Giving Index</a>, registering high levels of contentment and charitable behaviour in its society.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> In 2010, <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> placed Sri Lanka at the top of its list of 31 places to visit.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/S%26P_Dow_Jones_Indices" title="S&amp;P Dow Jones Indices">S&amp;P Dow Jones Indices</a> classifies Sri Lanka as a <a href="/wiki/Frontier_markets" title="Frontier markets">frontier market</a> as of 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index" title="List of countries by Human Development Index">Sri Lanka ranks</a> well above other South Asian countries in the <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> (HDI) with an index of 0.750.
</p><p>By 2016, the country's debt soared as it was developing its infrastructure to the point of near bankruptcy which required a bailout from the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> (IMF).<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> The IMF had agreed to provide a US$1.5&#160;billion bailout loan in April 2016 after Sri Lanka provided a set of criteria intended to improve its economy. By the fourth quarter of 2016, the debt was estimated to be $64.9&#160;billion. Additional debt had been incurred in the past by state-owned organisations and this was said to be at least $9.5&#160;billion. Since early 2015, domestic debt increased by 12% and external debt by 25%.<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> In November 2016, the IMF reported that the initial disbursement was larger than US$150&#160;million originally planned, a full US$162.6&#160;million (SDR 119.894&#160;million). The agency's evaluation for the first tranche was cautiously optimistic about the future. Under the program, the Sri Lankan government implemented a new Inland Revenue Act and an automatic fuel pricing formula which was noted by the IMF in its fourth review. In 2018 China agreed to bail out Sri Lanka with a loan of $1.25&#160;billion to deal with foreign debt repayment spikes in 2019 to 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2021, Sri Lanka declared a major <a href="/wiki/Economic_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic crisis">economic crisis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_2021_crisis_281-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_2021_crisis-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> The Chief of its Central Bank has stepped down amid the crisis.<sup id="cite_ref-Bloomberg_2021_282-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bloomberg_2021-282">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> The Parliament has declared emergency regulations due to the crisis, seeking to ban "food hoarding".<sup id="cite_ref-Al_Jazeera_2021_crisis_283-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Al_Jazeera_2021_crisis-283">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tourism, which provided the economy with an input of foreign currency, has been significantly impacted as a result of the ongoing COVID-19 pandemic.<sup id="cite_ref-The_New_York_Times_2021sri_285-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_New_York_Times_2021sri-285">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Transport in Sri Lanka">Transport in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>Sri Lanka has an extensive road network for inland transportation. With more than 100,000&#160;km (62,000&#160;mi) of paved roads,<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> it has one of the highest road densities in the world (1.5&#160;km or 0.93&#160;mi of paved roads per every 1&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 0.39&#160;sq&#160;mi of land). The road network consists of 35 <a href="/wiki/List_of_A-Grade_highways_in_Sri_Lanka" title="List of A-Grade highways in Sri Lanka">A-Grade highways</a> and <a href="/wiki/Expressways_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Expressways of Sri Lanka">four</a> <a href="/wiki/Controlled-access_highway" title="Controlled-access highway">controlled-access highways</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-288" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-288">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup> A and B grade roads are national (arterial) highways administered by Road Development Authority.<sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> C and D grade roads are provincial roads coming under the purview of the Provincial Road Development Authority of the respective province. The other roads are local roads falling under local government authorities.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Railways" title="Sri Lanka Railways">railway network</a>, operated by the state-run <a href="/wiki/National_Railway" title="National Railway">National Railway</a> operator <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Railways" title="Sri Lanka Railways">Sri Lanka Railways</a>, spans 1,447 kilometres (900&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-290" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-290">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka also has three <a href="/wiki/Deep-water_port" class="mw-redirect" title="Deep-water port">deep-water ports</a> at <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Colombo" title="Port of Colombo">Colombo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Galle" title="Galle">Galle</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Trincomalee" title="Trincomalee">Trincomalee</a>, in addition to the newest port being built at <a href="/wiki/Ruhunu_Magampura_International_Port" class="mw-redirect" title="Ruhunu Magampura International Port">Hambantota</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transition_to_biological_agriculture">Transition to biological agriculture</span></h3>
<p>In June 2021,  Sri Lanka imposed a nationwide ban on inorganic fertilisers and pesticides. The program was welcomed by its advisor <a href="/wiki/Vandana_Shiva" title="Vandana Shiva">Vandana Shiva</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-291" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> but ignored critical voices from scientific and farming community who warned about possible collapse of farming,<sup id="cite_ref-:7_292-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-292">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-296" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-296">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> including financial crisis due to devaluation of national currency pivoted around tea industry.<sup id="cite_ref-:7_292-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-292">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup> The situation in the tea industry was described as critical, with farming under the organic program being described as ten times more expensive and producing half of the yield by the farmers.<sup id="cite_ref-297" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-297">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup> In September 2021 the government declared an economic emergency, as the situation was further aggravated by falling national currency exchange rate, inflation rising as a result of high food prices, and pandemic restrictions in tourism which further decreased country's income.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_2021_crisis_281-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_2021_crisis-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 2021, Sri Lanka abandoned its plan to become the world's first organic farming nation following rising food prices and weeks of protests against the plan.<sup id="cite_ref-298" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-298">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup> As of December 2021, the damage to agricultural production was already done, with prices having risen substantially for vegetables in Sri Lanka, and time needed to recover from the crisis. The ban on fertiliser has been lifted for certain crops, but the price of urea has risen internationally due to the price for oil and gas.<sup id="cite_ref-The_New_York_Times_2021sri_285-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_New_York_Times_2021sri-285">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> Jeevika Weerahewa, a senior lecturer at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Peradeniya" title="University of Peradeniya">University of Peradeniya</a>, predicted that the ban would reduce the paddy harvest in 2022 by an unprecedented 50%.<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Demographics of Sri Lanka">Demographics of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:242px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SL_population_growth.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/SL_population_growth.png/240px-SL_population_growth.png" decoding="async" width="240" height="185" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/SL_population_growth.png/360px-SL_population_growth.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/SL_population_growth.png/480px-SL_population_growth.png 2x" data-file-width="781" data-file-height="603" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SL_population_growth.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sri Lanka's population, (1871–2001)</div></div></div><p>
Sri Lanka has roughly 22,156,000 people and an annual population growth rate of 0.5%. The <a href="/wiki/Birth_rate" title="Birth rate">birth rate</a> is 13.8 births per 1,000&#160;people, and the <a href="/wiki/Mortality_rate" title="Mortality rate">death rate</a> is 6.0 deaths per 1,000&#160;people.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> Population density is highest in western Sri Lanka, especially in and around the capital. <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_people" title="Sinhalese people">Sinhalese</a> constitute the largest ethnic group in the country, with 74.8% of the total population.<sup id="cite_ref-CPH2011_1_300-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CPH2011_1-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Tamils" title="Sri Lankan Tamils">Sri Lankan Tamils</a> are the second major ethnic group in the island, with a percentage of 11.2%. <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Moors" title="Sri Lankan Moors">Moors</a> comprise 9.2%. There are also small ethnic groups such as the <a href="/wiki/Burgher_people" title="Burgher people">Burghers</a> (of mixed European descent) and <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_Malays" title="Sri Lankan Malays">Malays</a> from Southeast Asia. Moreover, there is a small population of <a href="/wiki/Vedda_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Vedda people">Vedda people</a> who are believed to be the original indigenous group to inhabit the island.<sup id="cite_ref-301" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-301">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
