<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Languages of Sri Lanka">Languages of Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Sinhala_language" title="Sinhala language">Sinhala</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a> are the two <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official languages</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> The constitution defines <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_English" title="Sri Lankan English">English</a> as the link language. English is widely used for education, scientific and commercial purposes. Members of the <a href="/wiki/Burgher_people" title="Burgher people">Burgher</a> community speak variant forms of <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Creole" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese Creole">Portuguese Creole</a> and Dutch with varying proficiency, while members of the Malay community speak a form of <a href="/wiki/Creole_language" title="Creole language">Creole</a> <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Malay" class="mw-redirect" title="Sri Lanka Malay">Malay</a> that is unique to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-304" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Religion in Sri Lanka">Religion in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 31830.883898555px; border-left-color:Gray"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Gray"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Gray"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 15915.284869129px; border-left-color:Blue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Blue"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Blue"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 196.26105055051px; border-left-color:Green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Green"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:Green"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 53.350177136782px; border-left-color:DarkOrange"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:DarkOrange"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:DarkOrange"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:100px; border-width:95.486454474664px 29.704158157703px 0 0; border-top-color:Gold"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:Gold"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Sri Lanka (2012 census)<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2011census_306-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011census-306">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gold; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Buddhism_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Buddhism in Sri Lanka">Buddhism</a> (70.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:DarkOrange; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Hinduism_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Hinduism in Sri Lanka">Hinduism</a> (12.6%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Islam in Sri Lanka">Islam</a> (9.7%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Blue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Christianity in Sri Lanka">Christianity</a> (7.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gray; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Others (0.05%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> is the largest and is considered as an "<a href="/wiki/Official_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Official religion">Official religion</a>" of Sri Lanka under Chapter II, Article 9, "The Republic of Sri Lanka shall give to Buddhism the foremost place and accordingly it shall be the duty of the State to protect and foster the Buddha Sasana".<sup id="cite_ref-lawnet.gov.lk_307-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lawnet.gov.lk-307">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-308" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-308">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup>
Buddhism is practised by 70.2% of the Sri Lankan population with most being predominantly from <a href="/wiki/Theravada" title="Theravada">Theravada</a> school of thought.<sup id="cite_ref-bisl-sn_309-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bisl-sn-309">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup> Most Buddhists are of the Sinhalese ethnic group with minority Tamils. Buddhism was introduced to Sri Lanka in the 2nd century BCE by venerable <a href="/wiki/Mahinda_(buddhist_monk)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahinda (buddhist monk)">Mahinda Maurya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bisl-sn_309-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bisl-sn-309">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup> A sapling of the <a href="/wiki/Bodhi_Tree" title="Bodhi Tree">Bodhi Tree</a> under which <a href="/wiki/The_Buddha" title="The Buddha">the Buddha</a> attained enlightenment was brought to Sri Lanka during the same time. The <a href="/wiki/P%C4%81li_Canon" class="mw-redirect" title="Pāli Canon">Pāli Canon</a> (<i>Thripitakaya</i>), having previously been preserved as an oral tradition, was first committed to writing in Sri Lanka around 30 BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-ph-tbas_310-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ph-tbas-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka has the longest continuous history of Buddhism of any predominantly Buddhist nation.<sup id="cite_ref-bisl-sn_309-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bisl-sn-309">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup> During periods of decline, the Sri Lankan monastic lineage was revived through contact with Thailand and Burma.<sup id="cite_ref-ph-tbas_310-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ph-tbas-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although <a href="/wiki/Hinduism_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Hinduism in Sri Lanka">Hindus in Sri Lanka</a> a religious minority, Hinduism has been present in Sri Lanka at least since the 2nd century BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-311" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-311">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup> Hinduism was the dominant religion in Sri Lanka before the arrival of Buddhism in the 3rd century BCE. Buddhism was introduced into Sri Lanka by Mahinda, the son of Emperor Ashoka, during the reign of King <a href="/wiki/Devanampiya_Tissa" class="mw-redirect" title="Devanampiya Tissa">Devanampiya Tissa</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-312" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-312">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup> the Sinhalese embraced Buddhism and Tamils remain Hindus in Sri Lanka. However, it was activity from across the <a href="/wiki/Palk_Strait" title="Palk Strait">Palk Strait</a> that truly set the scene for <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a>'s survival in Sri Lanka.  <a href="/wiki/Shaivism" title="Shaivism">Shaivism</a> (devotional worship of Lord Shiva) was the dominant branch practised by the Tamil peoples, thus most of the traditional <a href="/wiki/Hindu_temple_architecture" title="Hindu temple architecture">Hindu temple architecture</a> and <a href="/wiki/Philosophy" title="Philosophy">philosophy</a> of Sri Lanka drew heavily from this particular strand of <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a>. Thirugnanasambanthar mentioned the names of several Sri Lankan Hindu temples in his works.<sup id="cite_ref-313" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-313">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Islam in Sri Lanka">Islam</a> is the third most prevalent religion in the country, having first been brought to the island by Arab traders over the course of many centuries, starting around the mid or late 7th century CE. Most followers on the island today are <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni</a> who follow the <a href="/wiki/Shafi%CA%BDi_school" class="mw-redirect" title="Shafiʽi school">Shafi'i school</a><sup id="cite_ref-314" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-314">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup> and are believed to be descendants of Arab traders and the local women whom they married.<sup id="cite_ref-315" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-315">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Christianity in Sri Lanka">Christianity</a> reached the country at least as early as the fifth century (and possibly in the first),<sup id="cite_ref-316" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-316">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup> gaining a wider foothold through Western colonists who began to arrive early in the 16th century.<sup id="cite_ref-317" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-317">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup> Around 7.4% of the Sri Lankan population are Christians, of whom 82% are <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Catholic Church in Sri Lanka">Roman Catholics</a> who trace their religious heritage directly to the Portuguese. Tamil Catholics attribute their religious heritage to <a href="/wiki/Francis_Xavier" title="Francis Xavier">St. Francis Xavier</a> as well as Portuguese missionaries. The remaining Christians are evenly split between the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Ceylon" title="Church of Ceylon">Anglican Church of Ceylon</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestant denominations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-318" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-318">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is also a small population of <a href="/wiki/Zoroastrian" class="mw-redirect" title="Zoroastrian">Zoroastrian</a> immigrants from India (<a href="/wiki/Parsis" title="Parsis">Parsis</a>) who settled in Ceylon during the period of British rule.<sup id="cite_ref-319" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-319">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup> This community has steadily dwindled in recent years.<sup id="cite_ref-320" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-320">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Religion plays a prominent role in the life and culture of Sri Lankans. The <a href="/wiki/Buddhist" class="mw-redirect" title="Buddhist">Buddhist</a> majority observe <a href="/wiki/Poya" title="Poya">Poya</a> Days each month according to the <a href="/wiki/Lunar_calendar" title="Lunar calendar">Lunar calendar</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hindus" title="Hindus">Hindus</a> and <a href="/wiki/Muslims" title="Muslims">Muslims</a> also observe their own holidays. In a 2008 <a href="/wiki/Gallup_poll" class="mw-redirect" title="Gallup poll">Gallup poll</a>, Sri Lanka was ranked the third most religious country in the world, with 99% of Sri Lankans saying religion was an important part of their daily life.<sup id="cite_ref-gallu_321-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gallu-321">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg/220px-Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg/330px-Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg/440px-Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Sri_Lanka.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Development of life expectancy</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Health in Sri Lanka">Health in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>Sri Lankans have a <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> of 75.5 years at birth, which is 10% higher than the world average.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-treasry_263-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treasry-263">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> The infant mortality rate stands at 8.5 per 1,000 births and the maternal mortality rate at 0.39 per 1,000 births, which is on par with figures from developed countries. The universal "pro-poor"<sup id="cite_ref-322" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-322">&#91;322&#93;</a></sup> health care system adopted by the country has contributed much towards these figures.<sup id="cite_ref-323" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-323">&#91;323&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka ranks first among southeast Asian countries with respect to deaths by suicide, with 33 deaths per 100,000 persons. According to the Department of Census and Statistics, poverty, destructive pastimes, and inability to cope with stressful situations are the main causes behind the high suicide rates.<sup id="cite_ref-324" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-324">&#91;324&#93;</a></sup>
On 8 July 2020, the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a> declared that Sri Lanka had successfully eliminated <a href="/wiki/Rubella" title="Rubella">rubella</a> and <a href="/wiki/Measles" title="Measles">measles</a> ahead of their 2023 target.<sup id="cite_ref-325" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-325">&#91;325&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Education in Sri Lanka">Education in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SLINTEC060917.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/SLINTEC060917.jpg/220px-SLINTEC060917.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="132" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/SLINTEC060917.jpg/330px-SLINTEC060917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/SLINTEC060917.jpg/440px-SLINTEC060917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2315" data-file-height="1384" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SLINTEC060917.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Institute_of_Nanotechnology" title="Sri Lanka Institute of Nanotechnology">Sri Lanka Institute of Nanotechnology</a> is a research institute specialising in the field of nanotechnology.</div></div></div>
<p>With a <a href="/wiki/Literacy_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Literacy rate">literacy rate</a> of 92.9%,<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka has one of the most literate populations amongst developing nations.<sup id="cite_ref-326" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-326">&#91;326&#93;</a></sup> Its youth literacy rate stands at 98.8%,<sup id="cite_ref-327" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-327">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup> computer literacy rate at 35%,<sup id="cite_ref-daynwsl_328-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-daynwsl-328">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup> and primary school enrollment rate at over 99%.<sup id="cite_ref-329" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-329">&#91;329&#93;</a></sup> An education system which dictates 9 years of <a href="/wiki/Compulsory_education" title="Compulsory education">compulsory schooling</a> for every child is in place.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Free_education" title="Free education">free education</a> system established in 1945<sup id="cite_ref-330" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-330">&#91;330&#93;</a></sup> is a result of the initiative of <a href="/wiki/C._W._W._Kannangara" title="C. W. W. Kannangara">C. W. W. Kannangara</a> and A. Ratnayake.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_331-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-331">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-332" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-332">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup> It is one of the few countries in the world that provide universal free education from primary to tertiary stage.<sup id="cite_ref-333" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-333">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> Kannangara led the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Madhya_Vidyalaya" title="Madhya Vidyalaya">Madhya Vidyalayas</a> (central schools) in different parts of the country in order to provide education to Sri Lanka's rural children.<sup id="cite_ref-daynwsl_328-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-daynwsl-328">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup> In 1942, a special education committee proposed extensive reforms to establish an efficient and quality education system for the people. However, in the 1980s changes to this system separated the administration of schools between the central government and the provincial government. Thus the elite <a href="/wiki/National_school_(Sri_Lanka)" title="National school (Sri Lanka)">national schools</a> are controlled directly by the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Ministry of Education (Sri Lanka)">ministry of education</a> and the provincial schools by the provincial government. Sri Lanka has approximately 10,155 government schools, 120 private schools and 802 <a href="/wiki/Pirivena" title="Pirivena">pirivenas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sri Lanka has 17 public universities.<sup id="cite_ref-334" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-334">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-335" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-335">&#91;335&#93;</a></sup> A lack of responsiveness of the education system to labour market requirements, disparities in access to quality education, lack of an effective linkage between secondary and tertiary education remain major challenges for the education sector.<sup id="cite_ref-336" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-336">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> A number of private, degree awarding institutions have emerged in recent times to fill in these gaps, yet the participation at tertiary level education remains at 5.1%.<sup id="cite_ref-337" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-337">&#91;337&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka was ranked 95th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 89th in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-338" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-338">&#91;338&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-339" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-339">&#91;339&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-340" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-340">&#91;340&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-341" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-341">&#91;341&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Science fiction author <a href="/wiki/Arthur_C._Clarke" title="Arthur C. Clarke">Arthur C. Clarke</a> served as chancellor of <a href="/wiki/Moratuwa_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Moratuwa University">Moratuwa University</a> from 1979 to 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-342" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-342">&#91;342&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights_and_media">Human rights and media</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Human rights in Sri Lanka">Human rights in Sri Lanka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Media_in_Sri_Lanka" class="mw-redirect" title="Media in Sri Lanka">Media in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Broadcasting_Corporation" title="Sri Lanka Broadcasting Corporation">Sri Lanka Broadcasting Corporation</a> (formerly <a href="/wiki/Radio_Ceylon" title="Radio Ceylon">Radio Ceylon</a>) is the oldest-running radio station in Asia,<sup id="cite_ref-slbcv_343-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slbcv-343">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup> established in 1923 by <a href="/wiki/Edward_Harper_(engineer)" title="Edward Harper (engineer)">Edward Harper</a> just three years after broadcasting began in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-slbcv_343-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slbcv-343">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup> The station broadcasts services in Sinhala, Tamil, English and Hindi. Since the 1980s, many private radio stations have also been introduced. Broadcast television was introduced in 1979 when the <a href="/wiki/Independent_Television_Network" title="Independent Television Network">Independent Television Network</a> was launched. Initially, all television stations were state-controlled, but private television networks began broadcasting in 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-344" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-344">&#91;344&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sri_Lanka&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 192 newspapers (122 Sinhala, 24 Tamil, 43 English, 3 multilingual) are published and 25 TV stations and 58 radio stations are in operation.<sup id="cite_ref-Annual_Report_2020_264-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annual_Report_2020-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> In recent years, <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press" title="Freedom of the press">freedom of the press</a> in Sri Lanka has been alleged by media freedom groups to be amongst the <a href="/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index" title="Press Freedom Index">poorest in democratic countries</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-345" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-345">&#91;345&#93;</a></sup> Alleged abuse of a newspaper editor by a senior government minister<sup id="cite_ref-You_are_pigs_who_eat_shit_346-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-You_are_pigs_who_eat_shit-346">&#91;346&#93;</a></sup> achieved international notoriety because of the unsolved murder of the editor's predecessor, <a href="/wiki/Lasantha_Wickrematunge" title="Lasantha Wickrematunge">Lasantha Wickrematunge</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Death_foretold_347-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Death_foretold-347">&#91;347&#93;</a></sup> who had been a critic of the government and had presaged his own death in a posthumously published article.<sup id="cite_ref-pre-written_posthumous_editorial_348-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pre-written_posthumous_editorial-348">&#91;348&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Officially, the constitution of Sri Lanka guarantees human rights as ratified by the United Nations. However, several groups, such as <a href="/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International">Amnesty International</a>, <a href="/wiki/Freedom_from_Torture" title="Freedom from Torture">Freedom from Torture</a>, <a href="/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch">Human Rights Watch</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-349" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-349">&#91;349&#93;</a></sup> as well as the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Government of the United Kingdom">British government</a><sup id="cite_ref-350" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-350">&#91;350&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">United States Department of State</a> have criticised human rights violations in Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-351" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-351">&#91;351&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Government of Sri Lanka">Sri Lankan Government</a> and the LTTE have both been accused of violating human rights. A <a href="/wiki/Report_of_the_Secretary-General%27s_Panel_of_Experts_on_Accountability_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Report of the Secretary-General&#39;s Panel of Experts on Accountability in Sri Lanka">report by an advisory panel to the UN secretary-general</a> accused both the LTTE and the Sri Lankan government of <a href="/wiki/Alleged_war_crimes_during_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Alleged war crimes during the Sri Lankan Civil War">war crimes</a> during final stages of the civil war.<sup id="cite_ref-352" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-352">&#91;352&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-353" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-353">&#91;353&#93;</a></sup> Corruption remains a problem in Sri Lanka, and there is little protection for those who stand up against corruption.<sup id="cite_ref-354" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-354">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup> The 135-year-old <a href="/wiki/Article_365_of_the_Sri_Lankan_Penal_Code" title="Article 365 of the Sri Lankan Penal Code">Article 365 of the Sri Lankan Penal Code</a> criminalises <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Sri_Lanka" title="LGBT rights in Sri Lanka">gay sex</a>, with a penalty of up to ten years in prison.<sup id="cite_ref-355" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-355">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Council" title="United Nations Human Rights Council">UN Human Rights Council</a> has documented <a href="/wiki/Enforced_disappearances_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Enforced disappearances in Sri Lanka">over 12,000 named individuals</a> who have disappeared after detention by security forces in Sri Lanka, the second-highest figure in the world since the Working Group came into being in 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-UNHRC_involuntary_disappearances_report_356-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNHRC_involuntary_disappearances_report-356">&#91;356&#93;</a></sup> The Sri Lankan government confirmed that 6,445 of these died. Allegations of human rights abuses have not ended with the close of the ethnic conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-People_are_disappearing_357-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-People_are_disappearing-357">&#91;357&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Office_of_the_United_Nations_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights" title="Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights">UN Human Rights Commissioner</a> <a href="/wiki/Navanethem_Pillay" class="mw-redirect" title="Navanethem Pillay">Navanethem Pillay</a> visited Sri Lanka in May 2013. After her visit, she said: "The war may have ended [in Sri Lanka], but in the meantime, democracy has been undermined and the rule of law eroded." Pillay spoke about the military's increasing involvement in civilian life and reports of military <a href="/wiki/Land_grabbing" title="Land grabbing">land grabbing</a>. She also said that, while in Sri Lanka, she had been allowed to go wherever she wanted, but that Sri Lankans who came to meet her were harassed and intimidated by security forces.<sup id="cite_ref-358" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-358">&#91;358&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-359" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-359">&#91;359&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2012, the UK charity <a href="/wiki/Freedom_from_Torture" title="Freedom from Torture">Freedom from Torture</a> reported that it had received 233 referrals of torture survivors from Sri Lanka for clinical treatment or other services provided by the charity. In the same year, the group published <i>Out of the Silence</i>, which documents evidence of torture in Sri Lanka and demonstrates that the practice has continued long after the end of the civil war in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-360" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-360">&#91;360&#93;</a></sup> On 29 July 2020, <a href="/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch">Human Rights Watch</a> said that the Sri Lanka government has targeted lawyers, human rights defenders, and journalists to suppress criticism against the government.<sup id="cite_ref-361" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-361">&#91;361&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG/220px-Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="173" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG/330px-Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG/440px-Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2559" data-file-height="2010" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vavuniya_Kavadi.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Hindu devotees engaging in <i><a href="/wiki/Kavadi" class="mw-redirect" title="Kavadi">Kavadi</a></i> at a temple in <a href="/wiki/Vavuniya" title="Vavuniya">Vavuniya</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Culture of Sri Lanka">culture of Sri Lanka</a> is influenced primarily by Buddhism and Hinduism.<sup id="cite_ref-precol_362-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-precol-362">&#91;362&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka is the home to two main traditional cultures: the Sinhalese (centred in Kandy and Anuradhapura) and the Tamil (centred in Jaffna). Tamils co-existed with the Sinhalese people since then, and the early mixing rendered the two ethnic groups almost physically indistinguishable.<sup id="cite_ref-363" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-363">&#91;363&#93;</a></sup> Ancient Sri Lanka is marked for its genius in <a href="/wiki/Ancient_constructions_of_Sri_Lanka#Irrigation_Works" title="Ancient constructions of Sri Lanka">hydraulic engineering</a> and <a href="/wiki/Architecture_of_ancient_Sri_Lanka" title="Architecture of ancient Sri Lanka">architecture</a>. The British colonial culture has also influenced the locals. The rich cultural traditions shared by all Sri Lankan cultures is the basis of the country's long life expectancy, advanced health standards and high literacy rate.<sup id="cite_ref-nub94_364-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nub94-364">&#91;364&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Food_and_festivals">Food and festivals</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_cuisine" title="Sri Lankan cuisine">Sri Lankan cuisine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Festivals_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Festivals in Sri Lanka">Festivals in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg/220px-Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="275" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg/330px-Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg/440px-Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="1600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lankan_Rice_and_Curry.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Sri Lankan rice and curry</div></div></div>
<p>Dishes include rice and curry, <a href="/wiki/Puttu" title="Puttu">pittu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kiribath" title="Kiribath">kiribath</a>, wholemeal <a href="/wiki/Roti" title="Roti">roti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Idiyappam" title="Idiyappam">string hoppers</a>, wattalapam (a rich pudding of Malay origin made with <a href="/wiki/Coconut_milk" title="Coconut milk">coconut milk</a>, jaggery, cashews, eggs, and spices including cinnamon and nutmeg), <a href="/wiki/Kottu" title="Kottu">kottu</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Appam" title="Appam">appam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-simplysl_365-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-simplysl-365">&#91;365&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Jackfruit" title="Jackfruit">Jackfruit</a> may sometimes replace rice. Traditionally food is served on a <a href="/wiki/Plantain_(cooking)" class="mw-redirect" title="Plantain (cooking)">plantain leaf or lotus leaf</a>. Middle Eastern influences and practices are found in traditional Moor dishes, while Dutch and Portuguese influences are found with the island's Burgher community preserving their culture through traditional dishes such as <a href="/wiki/Lamprais" title="Lamprais">lamprais</a> (rice cooked in stock and baked in a banana leaf), <a href="/wiki/Breudher" title="Breudher">breudher</a> (Dutch holiday biscuit), and <a href="/wiki/Bolo_fiado" title="Bolo fiado">Bolo fiado</a> (Portuguese-style layer cake).
</p><p>In April, Sri Lankans celebrate the <a href="/wiki/Sinhalese_New_Year" title="Sinhalese New Year">Buddhist</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tamil_New_Year" class="mw-redirect" title="Tamil New Year">Hindu</a> new year festivals.<sup id="cite_ref-dersl_366-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dersl-366">&#91;366&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Esala_Perahera" class="mw-redirect" title="Esala Perahera">Esala Perahera</a> is a symbolic Buddhist festival consisting of dances and decorated elephants held in Kandy in July and August.<sup id="cite_ref-esalasl_367-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-esalasl-367">&#91;367&#93;</a></sup> Fire dances, whip dances, Kandyan dances and various other cultural dances are integral parts of the festival. Christians celebrate <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a> on 25 December to celebrate the birth of Jesus Christ and <a href="/wiki/Easter" title="Easter">Easter</a> to celebrate the resurrection of Jesus. Tamils celebrate <a href="/wiki/Thai_Pongal" class="mw-redirect" title="Thai Pongal">Thai Pongal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maha_Shivaratri" title="Maha Shivaratri">Maha Shivaratri</a>, and Muslims celebrate <a href="/wiki/Hajj" title="Hajj">Hajj</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ramadan" title="Ramadan">Ramadan</a>.
</p>
<h3><span id="Visual.2C_literary_and_performing_arts"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Visual,_literary_and_performing_arts">Visual, literary and performing arts</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Cinema of Sri Lanka">Cinema of Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Music of Sri Lanka">Music of Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dances_of_Sri_Lanka" class="mw-redirect" title="Dances of Sri Lanka">Dances of Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Theatre_of_Sri_Lanka" title="Theatre of Sri Lanka">Theatre of Sri Lanka</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lankan_literature" title="Sri Lankan literature">Sri Lankan literature</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_(16720359423).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg/220px-Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg/330px-Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg/440px-Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_%2816720359423%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4142" data-file-height="2755" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_Women_in_Traditional_Kandyan_Dress_Laugh_While_Waiting_as_Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_Sri_Lankan_Foreign_Minister_Samaraweera_(16720359423).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Female dancers in traditional <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_dance" title="Kandyan dance">Kandyan dress</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_(Lotus_Pond)_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG/220px-The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG/330px-The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG/440px-The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_%28Lotus_Pond%29_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG 2x" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="418" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_landmark_Nelum_Pokuna_(Lotus_Pond)_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Nelum_Pokuna_Mahinda_Rajapaksa_Theatre" title="Nelum Pokuna Mahinda Rajapaksa Theatre">Nelum Pokuna Mahinda Rajapaksa Theatre</a> was constructed as a major venue for the performing arts</div></div></div>
<p>The movie <i><a href="/wiki/Kadawunu_Poronduwa" title="Kadawunu Poronduwa">Kadawunu Poronduwa</a></i> (<i>The Broken Promise</i>), produced by <a href="/wiki/S._M._Nayagam" title="S. M. Nayagam">S. M. Nayagam</a> of Chitra Kala Movietone, heralded the coming of Sri Lankan cinema in 1947. <i><a href="/wiki/Ranmuthu_Duwa" title="Ranmuthu Duwa">Ranmuthu Duwa</a></i> (<i>Island of Treasures</i>) marked the transition of cinema from <a href="/wiki/Black-and-white" class="mw-redirect" title="Black-and-white">black-and-white</a> to colour. In recent years, movies have featured subjects such as family melodrama, social transformation and the years of conflict between the military and the LTTE.<sup id="cite_ref-easiancinem_368-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-easiancinem-368">&#91;368&#93;</a></sup> The Sri Lankan cinematic style is similar to <a href="/wiki/Bollywood" class="mw-redirect" title="Bollywood">Bollywood</a> movies. In 1979, movie attendance rose to an all-time high but has been in a steady decline since then.<sup id="cite_ref-denandind_369-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-denandind-369">&#91;369&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An influential filmmaker is <a href="/wiki/Lester_James_Peiris" class="mw-redirect" title="Lester James Peiris">Lester James Peiris</a>, who has directed a number of movies which led to global acclaim, including <i><a href="/wiki/Rekava" title="Rekava">Rekava</a></i> (<i>Line of Destiny</i>, 1956), <i><a href="/wiki/Gamperaliya_(film)" title="Gamperaliya (film)">Gamperaliya</a></i> (<i>The Changing Village</i>, 1964), <i><a href="/wiki/Nidhanaya" title="Nidhanaya">Nidhanaya</a></i> (<i>The Treasure</i>, 1970) and <i><a href="/wiki/Golu_Hadawatha" title="Golu Hadawatha">Golu Hadawatha</a></i> (<i>Cold Heart</i>, 1968).<sup id="cite_ref-lstjms_370-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lstjms-370">&#91;370&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lankan-Canadian poet <a href="/wiki/Rienzi_Crusz" title="Rienzi Crusz">Rienzi Crusz</a>, is the subject of a documentary on his life in Sri Lanka. His work is published in Sinhala and English. Naturalised Canadian <a href="/wiki/Michael_Ondaatje" title="Michael Ondaatje">Michael Ondaatje</a> is well known for his English-language novels and three films.
</p><p>The earliest music in Sri Lanka came from theatrical performances such as <i>Kolam</i>, <i>Sokari</i> and <i>Nadagam</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-cambrmu_371-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cambrmu-371">&#91;371&#93;</a></sup> Traditional music instruments such as <i>Béra</i>, <i>Thammátama</i>, <i>Daŭla</i> and <i>Răbān</i> were performed at these dramas. The first music album, <i>Nurthi</i>, recorded in 1903, was released through Radio Ceylon. Songwriters like <a href="/wiki/Mahagama_Sekara" title="Mahagama Sekara">Mahagama Sekara</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ananda_Samarakoon" title="Ananda Samarakoon">Ananda Samarakoon</a> and musicians such as <a href="/wiki/W._D._Amaradeva" title="W. D. Amaradeva">W. D. Amaradeva</a>, <a href="/wiki/Victor_Ratnayake" class="mw-redirect" title="Victor Ratnayake">Victor Ratnayake</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nanda_Malini" title="Nanda Malini">Nanda Malini</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clarence_Wijewardene" class="mw-redirect" title="Clarence Wijewardene">Clarence Wijewardene</a> have contributed much towards the progression of Sri Lankan music.<sup id="cite_ref-wmrhg_372-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wmrhg-372">&#91;372&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Baila" title="Baila">Baila</a> originated among <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Kaffirs" title="Sri Lanka Kaffirs">Kaffirs</a> or the Afro-Sinhalese community.<sup id="cite_ref-lnlypnlu_373-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lnlypnlu-373">&#91;373&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg/220px-Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg/330px-Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg/440px-Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="720" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sri_Lanka_traditional_drum.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A Low Country drummer playing the traditional <i>Yak Béra</i></div></div></div>
<p>There are three main styles of Sri Lankan classical dance. They are, the <a href="/wiki/Kandyan_dance" title="Kandyan dance">Kandyan dances</a>, low country dances and <a href="/wiki/Sabaragamuwa" class="mw-redirect" title="Sabaragamuwa">Sabaragamuwa</a> dances. Of these, the Kandyan style is most prominent. It is a sophisticated form of dance<sup id="cite_ref-dncen2_374-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dncen2-374">&#91;374&#93;</a></sup> that consists of five sub-categories: <i>Ves dance</i>, <i>Naiyandi dance</i>, <i>Udekki dance</i>, <i>Pantheru dance</i> and <i>18 Vannam</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-sldance_375-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sldance-375">&#91;375&#93;</a></sup> An elaborate headdress is worn by the male dancers, and a drum called <i>Geta Béraya</i> is used to assist the dancers to keep on rhythm.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_376-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-376">&#91;376&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The history of Sri Lankan painting and sculpture can be traced as far back as to the 2nd or 3rd century BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-lklibrform_377-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lklibrform-377">&#91;377&#93;</a></sup> The earliest mention about the art of painting on Mahāvaṃsa, is to the drawing of a palace on cloth using <a href="/wiki/Cinnabar" title="Cinnabar">cinnabar</a> in the 2nd century BCE. The chronicles have a description of various paintings in relic chambers of Buddhist stupas and in monastic residences.
</p><p>Theatre came to the country when a <a href="/wiki/Parsi_theatre" title="Parsi theatre">Parsi theatre</a> company from <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a> introduced <i>Nurti</i>, a blend of European and Indian theatrical conventions to the Colombo audience in the 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-sldance_375-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sldance-375">&#91;375&#93;</a></sup> The golden age of Sri Lankan drama and theatre began with the staging of <i>Maname</i>, a play written by <a href="/wiki/Ediriweera_Sarachchandra" title="Ediriweera Sarachchandra">Ediriweera Sarachchandra</a> in 1956.<sup id="cite_ref-theatresl_378-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-theatresl-378">&#91;378&#93;</a></sup> It was followed by a series of popular dramas like <i>Sinhabāhu</i>, <i>Pabāvatī</i>, <i>Mahāsāra</i>, <i>Muudu Puththu</i> and <i>Subha saha Yasa</i>.
</p><p>Sri Lankan literature spans at least two millennia and is heir to the <a href="/wiki/Aryan_race" title="Aryan race">Aryan</a> literary tradition as embodied in the hymns of the <a href="/wiki/Rigveda" title="Rigveda">Rigveda</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-lklinbr_379-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lklinbr-379">&#91;379&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/P%C4%81li_Canon" class="mw-redirect" title="Pāli Canon">Pāli Canon</a>, the standard collection of scriptures in the Theravada Buddhist tradition, was written down in Sri Lanka during the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Buddhist_council" title="Fourth Buddhist council">Fourth Buddhist council</a>, at the Alulena cave temple, <a href="/wiki/Kegalle" title="Kegalle">Kegalle</a>, as early as 29 BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-alulena_380-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-alulena-380">&#91;380&#93;</a></sup> Chronicles such as the <i>Mahāvaṃsa</i>, written in the 6th century, provide vivid descriptions of Sri Lankan dynasties. According to the German philosopher <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_Geiger" title="Wilhelm Geiger">Wilhelm Geiger</a>, the chronicles are based on Sinhala <a href="/wiki/Atthakatha" title="Atthakatha">Atthakatha</a> (commentary).<sup id="cite_ref-lklinbr_379-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lklinbr-379">&#91;379&#93;</a></sup> The oldest surviving prose work is the <i>Dhampiya-Atuva-Getapadaya</i>, compiled in the 9th century CE.<sup id="cite_ref-lklinbr_379-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lklinbr-379">&#91;379&#93;</a></sup> The greatest literary feats of medieval Sri Lanka include <i>Sandesha Kāvya</i> (poetic messages) such as <i>Girā Sandeshaya</i> (parrot message), <i>Hansa Sandeshaya</i> (swan message) and <i>Salalihini Sandeshaya</i> (myna message). Poetry including <i>Kavsilumina</i>, <i>Kavya-Sekharaya</i> (<i>Diadem of Poetry</i>) and proses such as <i>Saddharma-Ratnāvaliya</i>, <i>Amāvatura</i> (<i>Flood of Nectar</i>) and <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pujavaliya&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pujavaliya (page does not exist)">Pujāvaliya</a></i> are also notable works of this period, which is considered to be the golden age of Sri Lankan literature.<sup id="cite_ref-lklinbr_379-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lklinbr-379">&#91;379&#93;</a></sup> The first modern-day novel, <i>Meena</i> by Simon de Silva appeared in 1905<sup id="cite_ref-sldance_375-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sldance-375">&#91;375&#93;</a></sup> and was followed by several revolutionary literary works. <a href="/wiki/Martin_Wickramasinghe" title="Martin Wickramasinghe">Martin Wickramasinghe</a>, the author of <i><a href="/wiki/Madol_Doova" title="Madol Doova">Madol Doova</a></i> is considered the iconic figure of Sri Lankan literature.<sup id="cite_ref-martinw_381-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-martinw-381">&#91;381&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Sri_Lanka" title="Sport in Sri Lanka">Sport in Sri Lanka</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_clouds_roll_in.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/The_clouds_roll_in.jpg/220px-The_clouds_roll_in.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/The_clouds_roll_in.jpg/330px-The_clouds_roll_in.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/The_clouds_roll_in.jpg/440px-The_clouds_roll_in.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4928" data-file-height="3264" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_clouds_roll_in.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/R._Premadasa_Stadium" title="R. Premadasa Stadium">R. Premadasa Stadium</a> in <a href="/wiki/Colombo" title="Colombo">Colombo</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>While the <a href="/wiki/National_sport" title="National sport">national sport</a> is <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, by far the most popular sport in the country is <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-382" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-382">&#91;382&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">Rugby union</a> also enjoys extensive popularity,<sup id="cite_ref-383" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-383">&#91;383&#93;</a></sup> as do <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">netball</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">tennis</a>. Aquatic sports such as boating, surfing, swimming, kitesurfing<sup id="cite_ref-384" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-384">&#91;384&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">scuba diving</a> attract many Sri Lankans and foreign tourists. There are two styles of martial arts native to Sri Lanka: <a href="/wiki/Cheena_di" title="Cheena di">Cheena di</a> and <a href="/wiki/Angampora" title="Angampora">Angampora</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-385" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-385">&#91;385&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_national_cricket_team" title="Sri Lanka national cricket team">Sri Lanka national cricket team</a> achieved considerable success beginning in the 1990s, rising from <a href="/wiki/Underdog_(competition)" class="mw-redirect" title="Underdog (competition)">underdog</a> status to winning the <a href="/wiki/1996_Cricket_World_Cup" title="1996 Cricket World Cup">1996 Cricket World Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-386" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-386">&#91;386&#93;</a></sup> They also won the <a href="/wiki/2014_ICC_World_Twenty20" title="2014 ICC World Twenty20">2014 ICC World Twenty20</a> played in Bangladesh, beating India in the final. In addition, Sri Lanka became the runners-up of the <a href="/wiki/Cricket_World_Cup" title="Cricket World Cup">Cricket World Cup</a> in <a href="/wiki/2007_Cricket_World_Cup" title="2007 Cricket World Cup">2007</a><sup id="cite_ref-387" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-387">&#91;387&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup_Final" title="2011 Cricket World Cup Final">2011</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-388" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-388">&#91;388&#93;</a></sup> and of the <a href="/wiki/ICC_World_Twenty20" class="mw-redirect" title="ICC World Twenty20">ICC World Twenty20</a> in <a href="/wiki/2009_ICC_World_Twenty20" title="2009 ICC World Twenty20">2009</a> and <a href="/wiki/2012_ICC_World_Twenty20" title="2012 ICC World Twenty20">2012</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-389" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-389">&#91;389&#93;</a></sup> Former Sri Lankan <a href="/wiki/Off_spin" title="Off spin">off-spinner</a> <a href="/wiki/Muttiah_Muralitharan" title="Muttiah Muralitharan">Muttiah Muralitharan</a> has been rated as the greatest <a href="/wiki/Test_cricket" title="Test cricket">test match</a> <a href="/wiki/Bowler_(cricket)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bowler (cricket)">bowler</a> ever by <i><a href="/wiki/Wisden_Cricketers%27_Almanack" title="Wisden Cricketers&#39; Almanack">Wisden Cricketers' Almanack</a></i>,<sup id="cite_ref-390" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-390">&#91;390&#93;</a></sup> and four Sri Lankan cricketers ranked 2nd (<a href="/wiki/Kumar_Sangakkara" title="Kumar Sangakkara">Sangakkara</a>), 4th (<a href="/wiki/Sanath_Jayasuriya" title="Sanath Jayasuriya">Jayasuriya</a>), 5th (<a href="/wiki/Mahela_Jayawardene" title="Mahela Jayawardene">Jayawardene</a>) and 11th (<a href="/wiki/Tillakaratne_Dilshan" title="Tillakaratne Dilshan">Dilshan</a>) highest ODI run scorers of all time, which is the second best by a team. Sri Lanka has won the <a href="/wiki/Asia_Cup" title="Asia Cup">Asia Cup</a> in 1986,<sup id="cite_ref-391" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-391">&#91;391&#93;</a></sup> 1997,<sup id="cite_ref-392" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-392">&#91;392&#93;</a></sup> 2004,<sup id="cite_ref-393" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-393">&#91;393&#93;</a></sup> 2008<sup id="cite_ref-394" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-394">&#91;394&#93;</a></sup> and 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-395" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-395">&#91;395&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka once held the highest team score in all three formats of cricket.<sup id="cite_ref-396" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-396">&#91;396&#93;</a></sup> The country co-hosted the Cricket World Cup in <a href="/wiki/1996_Cricket_World_Cup" title="1996 Cricket World Cup">1996</a> and <a href="/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup" title="2011 Cricket World Cup">2011</a> and hosted the <a href="/wiki/2012_ICC_World_Twenty20" title="2012 ICC World Twenty20">2012 ICC World Twenty20</a>.
</p><p>Sri Lankans have won two medals at <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic Games</a>: one silver, by <a href="/wiki/Duncan_White" title="Duncan White">Duncan White</a> at the <a href="/wiki/1948_Summer_Olympics" title="1948 Summer Olympics">1948 London Olympics</a> for men's <a href="/wiki/400_metres_hurdles" title="400 metres hurdles">400 metres hurdles</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-397" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-397">&#91;397&#93;</a></sup> and one silver by <a href="/wiki/Susanthika_Jayasinghe" title="Susanthika Jayasinghe">Susanthika Jayasinghe</a> at the <a href="/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics" title="2000 Summer Olympics">2000 Sydney Olympics</a> for women's <a href="/wiki/200_metres" title="200 metres">200 metres</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-398" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-398">&#91;398&#93;</a></sup> In 1973, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Lafir" title="Muhammad Lafir">Muhammad Lafir</a> won the <a href="/wiki/IBSF_World_Billiards_Championship" title="IBSF World Billiards Championship">World Billiards Championship</a>, the highest feat by a Sri Lankan in a <a href="/wiki/Cue_sports" title="Cue sports">Cue sport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-399" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-399">&#91;399&#93;</a></sup> Sri Lanka has also won the Carrom World Championship titles twice in 2012, <a href="/wiki/2016_Carrom_World_Championship" title="2016 Carrom World Championship">2016</a><sup id="cite_ref-400" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-400">&#91;400&#93;</a></sup> and 2018, the men's team becoming champions and the women's team winning second place.
</p>

<li id="cite_note-86"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-86">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://press-files.anu.edu.au/downloads/press/p69411/pdf/book.pdf">"Astronesians Historical and Comparative Perspectives" Page 146</a> "Annual trade between China and India through the Malacca Straits had opened by about 200 BCE. Perhaps by that time Austronesian sailors were regularly carrying cloves and cinnamon to India and Sri Lanka, and perhaps even as far as the coast of Africa in boats with outriggers. Certainly they have left numerous traces in canoe design, rigs, outriggers and fishing techniques, and a mention in Greek literature (Christie 1957)."</span>

<li id="cite_note-:7-292"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-:7_292-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-:7_292-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://agrigateglobal.com/amp/reads/opinion/opinion-the-ban-on-chemical-fertilizer-and-the-way-forward-of-sri-lankan-tea-industry/">"Opinion | The ban on chemical fertilizer and the way forward of Sri Lankan Tea Industry"</a>. <i>Agrigate Global</i><span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">5 September</span> 2021</span>. <q>By diverting the attention of policymakers towards pointless nonscientific arguments instead of promoting such integrated management systems and high technological fertilizer production, will be only a time-wasting effort and meanwhile, the global demand for Ceylon Tea will generate diminishing returns. At present, there are about 500,000 direct beneficiaries from the tea industry and about 600 factories are operating around the country. In general, the livelihood of around 3 million people is directly and indirectly woven around the domestic tea industry.  The researchers and the experienced growers have predicted that a 50 percent reduction in the yield has to be anticipated with the ban of chemical fertilizer. The negative implication of this yield reduction is such that there is a risk of collapsing the banking sector which is centralized around the tea industry in the major tea growing areas including Ratnapura, Galle, Matara, Kaluthara, and Kegalle.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Agrigate+Global&amp;rft.atitle=Opinion+%7C+The+ban+on+chemical+fertilizer+and+the+way+forward+of+Sri+Lankan+Tea+Industry&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fagrigateglobal.com%2Famp%2Freads%2Fopinion%2Fopinion-the-ban-on-chemical-fertilizer-and-the-way-forward-of-sri-lankan-tea-industry%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASri+Lanka" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-UNHRC_involuntary_disappearances_report-356"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-UNHRC_involuntary_disappearances_report_356-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFUnited_Nations" class="citation web cs1">United Nations, Human Rights Council Nineteenth session. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.ohchr.org/Documents/HRBodies/HRCouncil/RegularSession/Session19/A-HRC-19-58-Rev1_en.pdf">"Report of the Working Group on Enforced or Involuntary Disappearances"</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">24 November</span> 2012</span>. <q><span class="cs1-kern-left"></span>'The original mandate derives from Commission on Human Rights resolution 20 (XXXVI) of 29 February 1980','Since its establishment, the Working Group has transmitted 12,460 cases to the Government; of those, 40 cases have been clarified on the basis of information provided by the source, 6,535 cases have been clarified on the basis of information provided by the Government, 214 cases were found to be duplications and were therefore deleted, and 5,671 remain outstanding.'<span class="cs1-kern-right"></span></q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Report+of+the+Working+Group+on+Enforced+or+Involuntary+Disappearances&amp;rft.pages=3%2C+113&amp;rft.pub=UN&amp;rft.aulast=United+Nations&amp;rft.aufirst=Human+Rights+Council+Nineteenth+session&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.ohchr.org%2FDocuments%2FHRBodies%2FHRCouncil%2FRegularSession%2FSession19%2FA-HRC-19-58-Rev1_en.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASri+Lanka" class="Z3988"></span></span>
